## Poincaré Metric and Walk Geometry

*Interpretive note. Status: interpretive. Provenance: adapted. Moved from kernel Appendix D in v2.3.*

### The Analogy

At αTilt = −1, the walk weight is $w_k = 1/k$. This is structurally analogous to the Poincaré metric's $1/y$ scaling in the upper half-plane model: the metric $ds = |dz|/y$ shrinks distances as height $y$ increases, so that steps at larger $y$ carry less metric weight. The walk's k-th step receives weight $1/k$, the harmonic series weighting.

The Farey tessellation of the upper half-plane (§9.4) is hyperbolic. The three isometry types of the hyperbolic plane — Hyperbolic, Elliptic, Parabolic — have a suggestive correspondence to the three walk regimes (Helix, Circle, Spiral) observed in Remark 9.4a.

### Limits of the Analogy

The connection is structural: the Poincaré metric weight $1/y$ and the harmonic series weight $1/k$ both arise from the logarithmic derivative of the scaling group. However, the identification is suggestive rather than rigorously derived. The step index $k$ is not literally the imaginary part of a point in the half-plane, and a formal identification would require constructing a map from walk steps to half-plane points that respects both the metric and the Farey adjacency structure. This is the content of Open Problem 25. (Open Problem 25 is the walk-regime ↔ isometry-class map. It is distinct from the unnumbered
additive/multiplicative register-unification research-track note; the shared $\pm 1$ glyph is not a
shared object.)

### What Would Make It Rigorous

A proof would need to:
1. Construct an explicit embedding of the walk trajectory into the Poincaré half-plane
2. Show that the walk weight $w_k = k^{\alpha_{\text{Tilt}}}$ transforms covariantly under the SL(2,ℤ) action on the half-plane
3. Demonstrate that the walk regime (Helix/Circle/Spiral) is determined by the trace of the associated Möbius transformation (|tr| > 2 Hyperbolic, |tr| = 2 Parabolic, |tr| < 2 Elliptic)

Until such a construction exists, the correspondence remains an organizing analogy, not a theorem.

## Reference Data

*Moved from kernel appendices in v2.3.*

### The 720 Goldbach Pairs

All 39 pairs (p, q) with p + q = 720, both prime, both > 6:

```
(11, 709), (19, 701), (29, 691), (37, 683), (43, 677),
(47, 673), (59, 661), (61, 659), (67, 653), (73, 647),
(79, 641), (89, 631), (101, 619), (103, 617), (107, 613),
(113, 607), (127, 593), (149, 571), (151, 569), (157, 563),
(163, 557), (173, 547), (179, 541), (197, 523), (199, 521),
(211, 509), (229, 491), (233, 487), (241, 479), (257, 463),
(263, 457), (271, 449), (277, 443), (281, 439), (311, 409),
(331, 389), (337, 383), (347, 373), (353, 367)
```

### Sub-S₆ Exceptions

Even integers requiring primes ≤ 6 for all Goldbach pairs:

| n | Goldbach Pairs | Reason |
|---|----------------|--------|
| 4 | (2, 2) | Both primes ≤ 6 |
| 6 | (3, 3) | Both primes ≤ 6 |
| 8 | (3, 5) | Both primes ≤ 6 |
| 10 | (3, 7), (5, 5) | At least one ≤ 6 |
| 12 | (5, 7) | 5 ≤ 6 |
| 16 | (3, 13), (5, 11) | 3, 5 ≤ 6 |

For all other even n ≥ 14: at least one pair (p, q) exists with p, q > 6.

**Change from v3.1.12 to v3.1.13:**

The v3.1.13 change is the **corrected merge of the ALG-N + ANL-2 ingestion onto the v3.1.12 base.** No new mathematical content; no v3.0 theorem statement modified; no Open Problem touched; no new external authorities; no new bibliography section. The change reconciles a stale-base merge artifact and produces a single coherent v3.1.13 kernel. Status: bookkeeping / audit-discipline / commit-correction.

**The stale-base problem.** The May 24, 2026 Opus 4.6 ingestion of three Algorithm Notes (ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3) and one Architecture Note (ANL-2 Free Navigation Extension) was performed against a v3.0 fold that pre-dated the v3.1.2–v3.1.12 splice cluster (Note C3e-II.1 v3.1.4, §10.8 atlas-symmetry-speedup v3.1.5, Rmk 5.7a classicality v3.1.6, the §II.23 Measurement-Instrument cluster v3.1.7, the winding-only reversal cluster v3.1.8, the OP18 partial-closure cluster v3.1.9, the Weyl-1918 / adaptive-shell pair v3.1.10, Rmk II.3a v3.1.11, Rmk II.3b v3.1.12). The merge ran on the wrong head and produced a stale-merge artifact when the v3.1.0 / v3.1.1 ingestion content was concatenated onto an existing v3.1.12 base: header still reading v3.1.1, a duplicate Part VII tail (one stale, one corrected), a duplicate ANL-2 bibliography entry within the stale tail, and a closing marker reading `end v3.1.1 kernel`. The body of the file *did* contain all v3.1.2–v3.1.12 splices, but the Part VII tail had been written twice — once against the v3.1 architecture-version view (27 open problems, ALG-N lumped one-liners, in-kernel detailed changelog) and once against the v3.1.12 architecture-version view (29 open problems, full ALG-N statement-status-dependencies-distinction blocks, version-history pointer to `bibliography_v3_1_13.md`).

**The four reconciliation edits.** (1) Header version v3.1.1 → v3.1.13 with status line rewritten to enumerate the v3.1.2–v3.1.12 theorem-tier splices preserved verbatim (Lem I.5.3, Rmk II.3a, Rmk II.3b, Rmk 7.1, Def/Lem 9.7o, Rmk 9.7p, Note C3e-II.1, the §II.23 Measurement-Instrument cluster, Prop 8.20j / Rmk 8.20j.1 / Rmk 8.20h.5 / Prop 8.20h.6, Rmk II.23.7) and to attribute v3.1.13 as the additive ingestion of ALG-N (§VII.2 promotion) + ANL-2 (V.4, V.14, VI.7 paragraphs + VII.2 anchor) on top of the v3.1.12 base. The duplicate ANL-2 bibliography entry was inside that stale region and is removed with it. **Change from v3.1.11 to v3.1.12:**

The v3.1.12 change inserts **Remark II.3b** (*Prime-Power Tensor Decomposition of the Signature Kernel*, unconditional) at Part II / II.3, immediately after Rmk II.3a. One additive kernel insertion — no v3.0 theorem statement, scope-split language, formula, verification table, or subsection count modified; no Open Problem touched; no new external authorities. The change lands in the additive line of v3.1.1–v3.1.11, elevating a tensor-decomposition consequence already present in Rmk II.3a's Cardinal identity paragraph into its own anchored statement with a recorded computational consequence (Stage 2 of the harmonic-readout cost ladder).

**The kernel addition.** Under exponent-vector ordering of both $\Sigma_N$ and $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ — each as the cube $\prod_i \{0, \ldots, a_i\}$ — the signature-indexed Ramanujan kernel factors as a Kronecker product: $C_N \cong \bigotimes_{p^a \,\Vert\, N} C_{p^a}$, equivalently $C_N(q, \sigma) = \prod_i C_{p_i^{a_i}}(e_i, s_i)$ with each factor a small $(a_i+1) \times (a_i+1)$ integer block. Proof is one paragraph from Rmk II.3a's well-definedness and Thm 8.19 multiplicativity. The computational consequence: successive one-axis contractions replace the dense $O(\tau(N)^2)$ matrix-vector product of Stage 1 with $O(\tau(N) \cdot \sum_i (a_i + 1))$ multiply-adds (Stage 2), yielding a gain factor $\tau(N)/\sum_i(a_i+1)$ that grows as $2^{\omega(N)-1}/\omega(N)$ for squarefree $N$ and proportionally for moderate exponents. Representative operation counts: $N = 720$, 3.0×; $N = 30030$, 5.3×; $N = 720720$, 15×; $N = 30030^2$, 40.5×. The three-stage cost ladder (Stage 0: flat $O(L \cdot \tau(N))$; Stage 1: aggregation $O(L) + O(\tau(N)^2)$; Stage 2: tensor contraction $O(L) + O(\tau(N) \cdot \sum_i (a_i+1))$) is recorded in the kernel at II.3.

**Scope.** The Kronecker factorization is classical (Thm 8.19 multiplicativity); the FVLT contribution is the alignment of three registers as the same exponent cube: Hasse product $P_{a_1+1} \square \cdots \square P_{a_r+1}$ (Thm K.2), signature cube $\prod_i \{0, \ldots, a_i\}$ (Rmk II.3a), and kernel tensor table $\bigotimes C_{p^a}$ (Rmk II.3b). This is the structural content of the elevation: not new arithmetic, but a sightline through three registers of one combinatorial substrate. The Cooley–Tukey positioning is stated as analogy (same-kind Kronecker structure, different data structure) and explicitly not as identity.

**Verification.** Companion script `verify_tensor_speedup_v2.py`: structural identity $C_N = \bigotimes C_{p^a}$ verified as numpy array equality through $N = 30030$; computational identity (dense application vs. tensor-axis contraction result-equal) confirmed across test cases. Both routes agree.

**Three-stack.** Chris/ChatGPT: named the structural alignment "same exponent cube in three registers" and proposed the kernel-ready Rmk II.3b formulation. Robin: directed the elevation framing (II.3a content stands; II.3b extracts the tensor consequence into its own anchor), audited the Cooley–Tukey analogy as map-not-territory, ruled on the option (a) elevation, commit. Claude: verified the structural identity and computational identity numerically through $N = 30030$ in `verify_tensor_speedup_v2.py`, drafted the remark.

**Cross-references.** Rmk II.3b depends: Rmk II.3a (signature register factorization, the routing identity this remark decomposes tensorially), Part II / II.3 (Hasse–harmonic pairing section, landing site), Thm 8.19 (Ramanujan-sum multiplicativity, supplies the factorization), Thm K.2 (Cartesian product structure, supplies the Hasse-product register). No cross-reference patches to other sections required; landing at II.3 immediately after Rmk II.3a. Per v3.1.4/v3.1.7/v3.1.8 convention, no VII.2 or VII.9 entries added.

**Change from v3.1.10 to v3.1.11:**

The v3.1.11 change inserts **Remark II.3a** (*Signature Register Factorization of the Harmonic Readout*, unconditional) at Part II / II.3, immediately after Rmk 1.5c. One additive kernel insertion — no v3.0 theorem statement, scope-split language, formula, verification table, or subsection count modified; no Open Problem touched; no new external authorities. The change lands in the additive line of v3.1.1–v3.1.10 and names the computational middle register through which signal mass reaches the harmonic channel.

**The kernel addition.** Let $N = \prod_i p_i^{a_i}$. The $q$-channel Ramanujan-Fourier inner product $S_q = \sum_n x(n) c_q(n)$ for $q \mid N$ factors through the *capped $N$-valuation signature space* $\Sigma_N := \prod_i \{0, \ldots, a_i\}$ as $S_q = \sum_\sigma X_\sigma C_q(\sigma)$, where $X_\sigma = \sum_{n :\, \sigma_N(n) = \sigma} x(n)$ is signature mass and $C_q(\sigma) = c_q(n)$ for any $n$ with $\sigma_N(n) = \sigma$ is the signature-indexed Ramanujan kernel. Well-definedness rests on the cap condition $e_i \leq a_i$ for $q \mid N$, which forces $\gcd(q, n)$ to be a function of $\sigma_N(n)$ alone — proof one line: $e_i \leq a_i \Rightarrow \min(e_i, v_{p_i}(n)) = \min(e_i, \min(v_{p_i}(n), a_i))$. The cardinal identity $|\Sigma_N| = \tau(N)$ surfaces the exponent cube as the shared domain of the divisor side and the sample-signature side; tensor decomposition $C_q(\sigma) = \prod_i C_{p_i^{e_i}}(\sigma_i)$ follows from Thm 8.19 multiplicativity, each factor an $(a_i+1) \times (a_i+1)$ integer block. The kernel $C(q, \sigma)$ is integer-valued (Ramanujan sums are integers).

**Architectural role: the third register.** The Hasse–harmonic pairing (Rmk 1.5c) names two readouts of the same divisor $q \mid N$ — Hasse coordinate node and supercharacter / Ramanujan channel. Rmk II.3a adds the missing computational middle register through which signal mass reaches the harmonic channel: *index register $\to$ signature register $\to$ harmonic / divisor register*. The Hasse diagram is where the divisor channel lives; the supercharacter is what the channel measures; the signature register is where signal mass is gathered before the measurement. Status unconditional for the identity; an empirical paragraph records the computational consequence (cost reduction from $O(L \cdot \tau(N))$ to $O(L) + O(\tau(N)^2)$, with browser-side memory footprint at $q = N = 901{,}800{,}900$ explicitly tagged as $\approx 7.2$ GB `Float64Array` / $\approx 3.6$ GB `Float32Array`) as benchmark-dependent. Scope guard: this is a *routing identity for finite FVLT computation*, not a new Ramanujan-sum identity.

**Verification.** Companion script `verify_well_definedness.py` mechanically confirms the load-bearing claim two independent routes: (A) *analytic* — gcd factorization through capped signatures, with the one-line cap-condition algebra; (B) *enumerative* — for each $N$ and each $q \mid N$, partition $\{1, \ldots, \min(2N, 10000)\}$ by $\sigma_N(n)$ and verify all $n$ with the same signature give the same $c_q(n)$. Path B executes 8,393,694 pairwise equi-signature checks across $N \in \{12, 30, 60, 360, 720, 1001, 5040, 30030, 901{,}800{,}900\}$ (all 729 divisors of $30030^2$ included), 0 errors. Cross-check on the analytic path: $\gcd(q, n) = \prod_i p_i^{\min(e_i(q), \sigma_N(n)_i)}$ verified by direct computation on 6,368 cases, 0 errors. Sanity count confirms $|\Sigma_N| = \tau(N)$ on each tested $N$. Both paths agree; routes-must-agree discipline satisfied.

**Version-collision correction at commit.** The patch was originally drafted as v3.1.9; pre-commit kernel scan caught that v3.1.9 (Open Problem 18 partial closure) and v3.1.10 (Adaptive Operating Shell — Weyl-1918 Scale Gauge) were already committed during the intervening session, and renumbered to v3.1.11 — same collision class as the v3.1.5-to-v3.1.7 renumber recorded inside v3.1.7's change-block, and as the v2.7.4 audit redirect from v2.7.3 / OP 28–29. The drafting session worked from a stale `kernel_v3_1_8_committed.md` base; the head was at v3.1.10 in the canonical `kernel_v3_1_full_fold.md` and in the bibliography's detailed-changelog series. **Change from v3.1.9 to v3.1.10:**

The v3.1.10 change lands the Ratioque v2.6 "Weyl-Miller Gauge Potential" formula into the v3.1.x kernel with the audit-discipline-required layer split between *structural framing* and *empirical schedule*. The change is a two-piece additive patch — no v3.0 theorem statement, scope-split language, formula, verification table, or subsection count modified, no Open Problem touched. No new external authorities.

**Provenance and the v2.6 → v3.1.x sharpening.** The formula $G(\sigma) = \ln(1 + k\sigma)$, $N_{\text{local}} = N_{\text{base}}(1 + G)$ originated from a Robin Miller + Gemini exploration of Weyl gauge theory in fall 2025 and was deployed as Axiom A2 ("Weyl-Miller Gauge Potential") in the Ratioque v2.6 corporate integrity monitor (November 30, 2025). The v2.6 framing tied the gauge to "depth into outer automorphism space" and to "the system changing which Monster group representation is active based on market volatility." Both framings are *superseded* by v3.1.9's explicit Out($S_6$) closure (Rmk 8.20h.5, order-2 discrete with three same-size class-pair swaps at sizes 15 / 40 / 120 plus the size-90 pair fixed) — Out($S_6$) is discrete, not a continuous depth coordinate; the Monster connection remains bibliography-OP-5 speculation. The Weyl-1918 structural reading survives the supersession: $G = \log \lambda$ is the scalar scale potential of Weyl's 1918 unification attempt, with associated connection one-form $A = dG$, and this reading underwrites the formula's log structure independently of the v2.6 framing. v3.1.10 records the survival.

**The two kernel additions.**

- **Remark 7.1** (Part II / II.12 Gauge structure, after the *Gauge structure as instrument architecture* paragraph, **interpretive**) records the Weyl-1918 scale gauge as a candidate continuous gauge structure on the breathing operating shell, complementary to §7's discrete prime-permutation gauge. *Scalar scale potential* $G = \log \lambda$ with *connection one-form* $A = dG$; coexistence with the permutation gauge noted (orthogonal: permutation holds $n_3$ fixed and shuffles axis labels at fixed lens, Weyl-scale holds axis labels fixed and scales $n_3$ across time); joint structure ($S_m \times \mathbb{R}_{>0}$ combined gauge) reserved for future scope. Distinction from the kernel's four discrete-orientation involutions (chirality $\chi$, Weyl reflection $w$, signed completion, $W$) made explicit, with the historical Weyl-naming clash acknowledged (Weyl reflection is Lie-theoretic / finite-reflection-group; Weyl-1918 scale gauge is from the unification attempt). Explicit *modeling-choice-not-theorem* status caveat. Provenance paragraph records the Gemini-Weyl origin and the v2.6 → v3.1.x sharpening.

- **Remark II.23.7** (Part II / II.23.7, new subsection after II.23.6 L/D signature protocol, **empirical; interpretive structural reading stated**) supplies the canonical schedule $n_3(t) = N_{\text{base}}(1 + \ln(1 + k\nu(t)))$ with: (i) anchored / finite-slope-at-zero / sub-linear properties; (ii) compatibility with II.23.2 via the algebraic-extension argument of Rmk 7.1 (II.23.2's proof requires integer $s, t_1$, not integer $n_3$); (iii) validity domain empirical $k \in [1.5, 3.0]$ with $k = 2$ canonical for daily-return SD calibration; (iv) v2.6 deployment record on four datasets (SPY 2020 COVID, SPY 2025 correction, DJI 2025 healthy, PLTR 2025 hypergrowth) with state-distribution and asset-class classification consistent across regimes; (v) $G_{\max}$ cap derivation as the structural boundary of the $1 + G \approx e^G$ small-$G$ linearization (verified: $\approx 26.4\%$ relative error at $G = 1$, $\approx 9\%$ at $G = 0.5$); (vi) alternative functional forms (pure exponential $e^{k\nu}$, polynomial $(1+k\nu)^p$, sigmoid) noted as admissible Weyl-1918 modeling options with empirical, not structural, selection of $1 + k\nu$.

**Chris audit pre-commit (three required wording patches, all applied).**

1. **Rmk 7.1 scalar-vs-one-form precision.** First draft called $G = \log \lambda$ "the Weyl-1918 gauge potential"; Chris's patch separates the scalar log-scale coordinate from the connection one-form: $G$ is the scalar scale potential / log-scale coordinate, $A = dG = d\log\lambda$ is the connection one-form. Same precision propagated to the corresponding Weyl-gauge-reading paragraph in Rmk II.23.7.

2. **II.23.2 scope wording.** First draft claimed II.23.2 is "unconditional at any real $n_3(t)$" — but the v3.1.7 lemma as committed states "$n_3 \geq 2$ an integer." Chris's patch replaces the claim with the explicit algebraic-extension statement: the same proof extends to positive real $n_3(t)$ because the conjugacy step only requires $e^{is t_1 \pi} = (-1)^{s t_1}$ for integer $s, t_1$, not integrality of $n_3$. In adaptive-shell deployments $n_3(t)$ functions as instrument denominator / operating shell, not as a literal divisor-atom index — exactly the register II.23.7 operates in.

3. **II.23.7 status-label normalization.** First draft status read *empirical / interpretive*. Chris's patch normalizes to *empirical; interpretive structural reading stated* — the HTML annotation `status: empirical` is correct (the schedule is empirical); the interpretive structural reading is referenced in prose where needed (e.g., the Weyl-1918 gauge reading paragraph).

**Two open questions closed by Chris audit.**

- **Status label for Rmk 7.1: *interpretive*.** Do not use *definitional* yet — Rmk 7.1 records a modeling frame for a continuous gauge, not a kernel-defined gauge group with worked-out joint invariants.
- **Out($S_6$) ↔ Weyl-scale-gauge speculation: silent.** Do not add a speculation paragraph connecting the v3.1.9 Out($S_6$) closure to the v3.1.10 Weyl-scale gauge. Keep silent at the kernel level; bibliography OP territory (parallel to OP 5 Langlands speculation) if revisited later.

**Verification.** Finite-claim two-route check on the $G = 1$ linearization error: closed form $(e - 2)/e$ gives $\approx 26.42\%$; Python script `python -c "import math; print(abs((1+1)-math.exp(1))/math.exp(1))"` returns the same. The cap rationale is therefore a structural fact about the linearization, not an arbitrary fudge factor — at $G = 1$ the small-$G$ approximation has visibly diverged from the exponential. At $G = 0.5$ the error is $\approx 9\%$, defining the natural range of cap choice $G_{\max} \in [0.5, 1.0]$.

**Status of the v2.6 framings.** The v2.6 document's "outer automorphism space" continuous-depth language is *superseded* (v3.1.9 Out($S_6$) closure makes this discrete). The Monster representation activation claim is *held to bibliography speculation* (OP 5 Langlands territory, unchanged from v2.7.x). The Yang-Mills analogy (gauge potential $A$, curvature $F = dA$, fiber bundle) is *partially recovered*: $A = dG$ in Rmk 7.1 is genuinely a Weyl-1918 connection one-form, but no curvature $F$ is computed, no fiber bundle constructed; the Yang-Mills vocabulary is therefore present at the connection-one-form level only. The v2.6 dual-grid Vernier structure (Grid_f = N−1, Grid_p = N+1) was already folded in at v3.1.7 (Lemma II.23.2 conjugate-pair structure + II.23.3 bracket taxonomy + Rmk II.23.3a mod-4 walls). The v2.6 wobble / clinamen (High − Low) / Brownian dimensional-reduction reading is already in the kernel from v2.7.13 (Part II / II.6 phasor collapse + clinamen + unitarity). The v2.6 rolling-percentile phronesis layer is application-level instrument tuning and stays out of the kernel.

**Three-stack provenance.** Robin (architect): the Weyl gauge theory exploration with Gemini (fall 2025); the Ratioque v2.6 deployment and four-dataset validation; the question of whether the formula still has structural content given the v3.1.x sharpening of $S_6$; the layer-split decision (structural framing in Rmk 7.1, empirical schedule in Rmk II.23.7); commit. Claude: Weyl-1918 gauge identification distinguishing it from the kernel's existing four discrete-orientation involutions; the $1 + G \approx e^G$ small-$G$ linearization-and-cap structural connection (cap is where linearization breaks down at $\approx 26\%$ relative error); the orthogonality reading of permutation-vs-scale gauges (axis labels held fixed by one, magnitude held fixed by the other); the two-piece split between Rmk 7.1 structural and Rmk II.23.7 empirical; Provenance paragraph naming the v2.6 framing supersession; commit motion execution. Chris/ChatGPT (audit): the scalar-potential-vs-connection-one-form precision; the II.23.2 scope catch (integer lemma scope, algebraic extension is what supports adaptive deployment); the status-label normalization; the *interpretive* (not *definitional*) ruling for Rmk 7.1; the *silent* (not *bibliography-OP*) ruling for the Out($S_6$) ↔ Weyl-scale-gauge speculation; CHRIS AUDIT PASS / ADAPTIVE OPERATING SHELL v3.1.10 CLEARED FOR COMMIT AS TWO-PIECE PATCH.

**Cross-references threaded at commit.** Rmk 7.1 depends: §7 (existing), Part IV / IV.1 (breathing lens), Part II / II.23.2 (Vernier conjugate-pair). Rmk II.23.7 depends: Rmk 7.1 (Weyl framework), Part II / II.23.2 (Vernier compatibility), Part IV / IV.1 (breathing-shell concept). Forward cross-references in Rmk 7.1 to II.23.7 (canonical schedule) and to Part I / I.3 + Def 9.7o (the kernel's four discrete-orientation involutions). No cross-reference paragraph patches to other sections required — the additions are self-contained within their landing locations.

ALG-2 is stated on the canonical reduced curve $t_1 = t_2 = 1$. - **v3.1.3** ($p_{\mathrm{null}}$-statistic clarification, May 18, 2026) — one additive point-version pinning the bridge statistic that $p_{\mathrm{null}}$ ranks. No v3.0 theorem statement modified; in the additive line of v3.1.1 / v3.1.2. §8.4a (Part V / V.8) defines $\mathrm{ce}_z$ (Def 8.20f), $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$ over $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$ (Rmk 5.5c), the $(\beta, p_{\mathrm{null}})$ diagnostic pairing (Rmk 8.20e), and surrogate validity (Rmk 8.20e.1, V.17) — but did not state **which scalar bridge statistic $p_{\mathrm{null}}$ ranks**. This point-version fixes that: **$p_{\mathrm{null}}$ ranks $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$** — the add-one rank of the observed $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$ among the $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$ values of a valid (Rmk 8.20e.1) surrogate ensemble. **The complement energy $E_C$ is not a valid $p_{\mathrm{null}}$ statistic:** $E_C$-ranked $p_{\mathrm{null}}$ is structurally prone to saturation against a structure-destroyed null — a signal with genuine arithmetic structure carries more complement energy than a structure-destroyed surrogate of itself, so the observed $E_C$ tends to exceed the whole surrogate band and $p_{\mathrm{null}}^{E_C}$ collapses toward the rank floor, a presence/absence detector rather than a resolution diagnostic. $E_C$ retains its role as the quantity $\mathrm{ce}_z$ standardizes (Def 8.20f) — saturation does not arise there, $\mathrm{ce}_z$ being a continuous $z$-score, not a rank. $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$, bounded in $[1/8,1]$ and a concentration rather than an extensive energy, has a non-degenerate rank and supplies the $(\beta, p_{\mathrm{null}})$ pair with a non-saturating partner. - **v3.1.4** (the prime-rank register, May 19, 2026) — one additive point-version adding **Note C3e-II.1** (*The Prime-Rank Register*) at Part II / II.18, after Note 9.7m. The note names the register intermediate between the profile register (the unordered exponent multiset $\{a_i\}$, all a single unweighted lattice shape delivers — $G_N$ a commutative Cartesian product of paths, Thm K.2) and the prime-identity register (the full labeled integer $N$): the *prime-rank register* records exponent behavior against prime-size order $p_1 < p_2 < \cdots$ without carrying named-prime identity. Load-bearing structural statement: *prime-rank and prime-support are independent refinements of profile, with prime-identity their join.* The note is interpretive (a register-discipline framing); its one classical anchor, $\mathbb{E}[v_p(n)] = 1/(p-1)$ for a random-integer ensemble, is elementary and unconditional, and the extremal-family pressure (non-increasing exponents on initial prime segments) is the anti-correlation rule already in Rmk 9.7l's scope-of-characterization. The note prevents a false binary — that a readout is either profile-level or identity-level — and bounds the §10.8(c) transport claim (v3.1.5, below): profile-class transport stops at the prime-rank register. Sequenced ahead of §10.8 (v3.1.5) so the dependency runs forward, not back — §10.8(c)'s corrected boundary depends on this note (Chris-recommended order swap). Naming kept as "Note C3e-II.1" from the originating C3e-II audit line; a §9.7-cluster designation (Note 9.7o) would be a one-line change but is declined per Chris audit — this is a register-discipline note, not a §9.7 geometry note. Unconditional core: *profile class (the exponent multiset $\{a_i\}$) $=$ isomorphism class of atoms* — Rmk 9.7f's single instance $\mathrm{Div}(30) \cong \mathrm{Div}(1001)$ made atlas-general — so any profile-register quantity transports across an entire (infinite) profile class without recomputation. §10.8(a) reads the III.1 signature-shape axis as a symmetry-speedup cost axis (interpretive); (b) states profile-class transport (unconditional core, operational transport reading); (c) bounds transport to the profile register; the instrument consequence (complement-loaded Stage-2 speedup of $\mathrm{Div}(720720)$) is unconditional; the Scaling block records the superprimorial product-vs-sum ratio $\tau(\mathrm{Sp}_n)/\sum_i(a_i+1) = (n+1)!/[n(n+3)/2]$ (unconditional; $\mathrm{Sp}_n$ as canonical extremal witness interpretive), with the rendering consequence held to companion-documentation scope. **§10.8(c) failure-boundary correction.** The earlier Chris-audited §10.8 draft glossed the transport-failure boundary as "any diagnostic depending on named primes rather than exponent profile." That gloss under-includes: a profile class mixes exponent-by-prime-size assignments — $\mathrm{Div}(2^4 \cdot 3^2 \cdot 5)$ and $\mathrm{Div}(3^4 \cdot 2^2 \cdot 5)$ both lie in profile class $(4,2,1)$, as §10.8's own *Concrete instances* list shows — so a quantity sensitive to which prime carries which exponent fails to transport even though it names no prime, contradicting the (correct) headline "valid exactly on the profile register." §10.8(c) and the §10.8 summary blockquote were corrected so the failure boundary is "every register finer than the profile register, the prime-rank register first," consistent with §10.8(b)'s relabeling-invariance definition and cross-referenced to Note C3e-II.1 (v3.1.4, above). - **v3.1.11** (signature register factorization of the harmonic readout, May 21, 2026) — one additive point-version inserting **Remark II.3a** (*Signature Register Factorization of the Harmonic Readout*, unconditional, Part II / II.3, immediately after Rmk 1.5c). No v3.0 theorem statement modified; in the additive line of v3.1.1–v3.1.10. The remark identifies the capped $N$-valuation signature space $\Sigma_N \cong \prod_i \{0, \ldots, a_i\}$ as the computational middle register through which signal mass reaches the harmonic channel $c_q$ for $q \mid N$: the Ramanujan-Fourier inner product $S_q = \sum_n x(n) c_q(n)$ factors as $S_q = \sum_\sigma X_\sigma C_q(\sigma)$ where $X_\sigma$ is signature mass and $C_q(\sigma)$ is the signature-indexed Ramanujan kernel. Well-definedness rests on the cap condition $e_i \leq a_i$ for $q \mid N$, forcing $\gcd(q, n)$ to be a function of $\sigma_N(n)$ alone — proof one line: $e_i \leq a_i \Rightarrow \min(e_i, v_{p_i}(n)) = \min(e_i, \min(v_{p_i}(n), a_i))$. Cardinal identity $|\Sigma_N| = \tau(N)$ surfaces the exponent cube as the shared domain of the divisor side and the sample-signature side; tensor decomposition $C_q(\sigma) = \prod_i C_{p_i^{e_i}}(\sigma_i)$ follows from Thm 8.19 multiplicativity, each factor an $(a_i+1) \times (a_i+1)$ integer block. The kernel $C(q, \sigma)$ is integer-valued (Ramanujan sums are integers). Adds the third register to the Hasse–harmonic pairing of Rmk 1.5c: *index $\to$ signature $\to$ harmonic*. Status unconditional for the identity; an empirical paragraph records the computational consequence (cost reduction from $O(L \cdot \tau(N))$ to $O(L) + O(\tau(N)^2)$, with browser-side memory footprint at $q = N = 901{,}800{,}900$ tagged as $\approx 7.2$ GB Float64 / $\approx 3.6$ GB Float32) as benchmark-dependent. Scope guard: routing identity for finite FVLT computation, not a new Ramanujan-sum identity. Companion script `verify_well_definedness.py` confirms the load-bearing claim two independent routes — analytic (gcd factorization through capped signatures) and enumerative (8,393,694 pairwise equi-signature checks across $N \in \{12, 30, 60, 360, 720, 1001, 5040, 30030, 901{,}800{,}900\}$, all 729 divisors of $30030^2$ included, 0 errors; gcd cross-check 6,368 cases, 0 errors). **Version-collision correction at commit.** The patch was originally drafted as v3.1.9; pre-commit kernel scan caught that v3.1.9 (OP18 partial closure) and v3.1.10 (Adaptive Operating Shell) had been committed during the intervening session, and renumbered to v3.1.11 — same collision class as the v3.1.5-to-v3.1.7 renumber. Per convention established by v3.1.4 / v3.1.7 / v3.1.8 kernel-internal additions, no VII.2 anchor-map or VII.9 bibliography-section entry added — kernel-internal remarks live at their landing location plus this compact-bullet record and the v3.1.10 → v3.1.11 detailed change-block above. Provenance: a Robin + Claude + Chris debugging cycle on `supercharacters.html` (May 21, 2026) — Robin's debugging pressure around large-N prime spectra and FVLT-native computation, Chris's correction of the hardcoded-2-chunks GPU framing and the period-table memory error, and the three-stack ratification of the factorization as the load-bearing claim with the speedup as a measured consequence. The kernel-safe form is *the harmonic readout factors through the capped valuation signature register*, not *we found a faster implementation*. Three-stack: Robin (architect; the architectural directive that the routing identity is the claim, the speedup the consequence; specified placement near II.2–II.3, title, status, depends list, and three in-place edits — `depends: 8.19`, "integer matrix" over "rational matrix", type-explicit memory footprints; commit), Claude (the debugging session including the Hölder-form transform replacement, the prime-N `Float64Array(Pr)` crash diagnosis, the GPU memory-vs-bandwidth analysis, and the verification harnesses; drafted Rmk II.3a and the change-block; the well-definedness proof and the 8.4M-pair verification), Chris/ChatGPT (correction of GPU-chunking and period-table claims; ratified the sharpened framing of signature aggregation as a routing identity rather than an acceleration heuristic; verdict PASS for insertion as Rmk II.3a, kernel-safe boundary preserved between unconditional identity and benchmark-dependent implementation paragraph).

**Change from v3.0 to v3.1:**

The v3.1 change is the phasor-side algorithmic core ingestion: three Algorithm Notes (ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3) added to the kernel as bibliography-tier readout instruments parallel to the v2.7.3 Architecture Notes. Additive only — no v3.0 theorem statement, scope-split language, formula, verification table, or subsection count modified. No new external authorities. One new Open Problem transposed (OP 30, recorded out-of-kernel since v2.7.4).

The three algorithm notes are phasor-side readouts of one object — the recursive Dirichlet/factorial construction — not separable components; they read the same divisor lattice at fixed integer spin as vertices, strata, and ratios respectively:

- **ALG-1** (Single-Phasor Divisor Gate for Hasse Atom Construction). At integer spin $s$, the single-phasor imaginary part vanishes exactly on the divisors of $s$: $\Im(z_1) = 0 \iff N \mid s$. Sweeping $N$ recovers $\mathrm{Div}(s)$ — the divisor-index set, equivalently the ALG-1 vertex set — with no graph reconstruction. Verification V1 + V2 PASS (Chris audit, May 13, 2026).
- **ALG-2** (Cyclotomic Stratification of the Single-Phasor Curve at Integer Spin). Off the real axis, single-phasor values at integer spin $s$ stratify by $d = \gcd(s, N)$ into $\tau(s)$ gcd-classes of size $\varphi(s/d)$, with three orders distinguished — Euler-residual $m_0$, plotted-phasor $m_{\mathrm{plot}}$, contact-normalized $m_1$. Experimental verification C1 (`run_c1.py`, `c1_verdict.md` — 0 violations at $s = 30, 720$; stratum counts and sizes confirmed) plus a three-orders precision pass.
- **ALG-3** (Continuous-Phasor Winding Recovery of Divisor Lattice Ratios). Along the continuous-$N$ phasor curve, the angular winding between two divisor nodes is $\Delta\theta = \pi\ell(r-1)$ with $r = N_2/N_1$, $\ell = s/N_2$; the winding recovers divisor-lattice ratios, with Hasse covers identified by a combinatorial atomicity test on $r$. The continuum enters as an integration variable along the phasor curve, not as an arithmetic object — the fractional-$N$ reformulation (Robin's redirect after the integer-$N$ search for Hasse-adjacency geometry was exhausted) answers RES-1 C2 as posed. Verification V1 + V2 PASS (Chris audit, May 15, 2026); experimental verification C2 (`run_c2.py`, `c2_verdict.md` — exact rational arithmetic, 0 violations across all 240 + 19 divisibility pairs at $s = 720, 30$).

- **Kernel cross-reference insertions.** 15 cross-reference paragraphs across 9 v3.0 sites — Part I / I.6 (ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3 at factorial grading); Part II / II.2 (ALG-2 at the harmonic / Ramanujan readout); Part II / II.3 (ALG-1 at the Hasse–harmonic pairing); Part II / II.5 (ALG-1, ALG-2, ALG-3 at Vernier interference); Part II / II.14 (ALG-2 + ALG-3 at the multi-prime polygonal embedding); Part V / V.2 (ALG-1 at the selection of $N_0$); Part V / V.4 (ALG-2 + ALG-3 at the basis–complement architecture, including the RES-2 closure note); Part V / V.14 (ALG-3 at spectral facts, with the C3 open question surfaced — "how does an angle integral see a Laplacian eigenvalue?"); Part VI / VI.7 (ALG-1 at mathematical visualization). **Change from v2.7.14 to v3.0:**

The v3.0 change is an architectural migration — the atlas-first full fold — not a version bump. v2.7.14 theorem content carries verbatim with zero new theory: every theorem, lemma, definition, corollary, remark, and note carries its statement and its v2.2-frozen status label unchanged; the single *proposed* item (§5.6 Variational Completion) remains proposed. No new external authorities. No new Open Problems.

- **Universe of discourse.** v2.7.14 treated $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ implicitly as "the instrument" and load-bearing as substrate. v3.0 generalizes: the substrate is the atlas $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{N}} = \{\mathrm{Div}(n) : n \in \mathbb{N}_{>0}\}$, and $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ is reframed as the canonical FVLT instrument *atom* — one atom in $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{N}}$, selected by named criteria (Thm 8.20h.3) — not the substrate. $\mathrm{Div}(720720)$ is a scaled exemplar of the canonical instrument family. Negative integers are handled by the signed completion $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times} = \{\mathrm{Div}^{+}(n), \mathrm{Div}^{-}(n)\}$, two oriented sheets of the positive atlas — not a single signed-index atlas; the phrase "atlas over $\mathbb{Z}$" is rejected as wrong and the distinction is enforced lexically and structurally. $0$ is structurally excluded from the atlas index set ($\mathrm{Div}_{\mathrm{literal}}(0)$ has infinitely many divisors) and retained as the additive carrier hub.
- **Document architecture.** The flat §0–§16 + Appendix K + Notation + References sectioning — which had grown organically across the v2.7.x series with $\mathrm{Div}(720)$-as-substrate baked into §1 through §13 — is reorganized into seven Parts, each answering one question: I Substrate (what exists?), II Instruments (how is the substrate measured?), III Atlases and Structural Classes (what kinds of atoms appear?), IV Dynamics and Walk Grammar (how do walks move?), V The Canonical FVLT Instrument Atom (why $\mathrm{Div}(720)$, now that it isn't substrate?), VI Applications / Tooling / AI (what is it for?), VII Status / Scope / Open Problems / Notation / Bibliography (what is proved, conditional, open?). 103 content-bounded subsections, distributed 10 / 22 / 16 / 13 / 17 / 14 / 11; no Part bleeds into another.
- **Dual-reference convention.** Every subsection in Parts I–VII carries a three-line header block — Source (v2.7.14 / v3.0-alpha origin labels), v3.0 location (Part X / X.N), Status (v2.2 frozen-vocabulary label). Original theorem / remark / note labels are preserved both at the subsection header and inline; v3.0 Part-number addresses are *added*, not substituted. The full fold is auditable against v2.7.14 in both directions.
- **Notation additions.** Part VII / VII.8 extends the v2.7.14 glossary with v3.0-specific entries, explicitly flagged as additions (not preserved-verbatim): $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{N}}$, $\mathcal{A}_{\mathbb{Z}}^{\times}$, $\mathrm{Div}^{+}(n)$, $\mathrm{Div}^{-}(n)$, the atlas order / meet / join $\leq_{\mathcal{A}} / \wedge_{\mathcal{A}} / \vee_{\mathcal{A}}$, the signature $\sigma(n)$ and atlas-displacement $\sigma(a)$, the basis/complement formal symbols $\mathcal{B} / \mathcal{C}$, the bridge prime-contribution decomposition $B(d) / C(d) / E(d)$, the bridge bias scalar $\beta_{\mathrm{bridge}}(d)$, the bridge posture tuple $\pi_{\mathrm{bridge}}(d)$. Old $B$, $B'$ working notation is retained as v2.7.14 carry-through.
- **Routing.** §C / §E / §F / §G remain routed to companion-spine; body-inclusion was post-v3.0 scope. §G core representation content is already covered by Part II / II.17 (Rmk 9.7j) + II.22 (§13 Fock space) + Part V / V.13 (§8.7 spinor–tensor). §9.4 was decomposed item-by-item rather than relocated as a unit. Lemma 9.3a and Note 9.3a.1 were kept together inside Part II / II.13.
- **Two pre-freeze micro-patches**, both catching genuine over-claims that survived the main draft: (1) the front-matter Basis line was corrected from identifying $S_6$ (a group) with $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ (an atom) to an atlas-first phrasing retaining the legacy $S_k / S_6$ frame as the canonical instrument shell; (2) the II.12 gauge-automorphism claim of a free $S_3$ on basis primes at $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ was corrected — $\mathrm{Div}(720) \cong P_5 \square P_3 \square P_2$ has unequal axis lengths $(5,3,2)$, and the asymmetric signature $(4,2,1)$ blocks the free $S_3$; the automorphism group on basis primes is trivial.
- **Bibliography.** Preserved verbatim at v3.0 (Part VII / VII.9). Reflow by macro-section deferred to v3.1+. This standalone `FVLT_Bibliography.md` companion was unchanged by v3.0 and is brought current at v3.1 (this update).
- **Deliverables.** Three files — `v3_0_full_fold_map.md` (binding fold map, every v2.7.14 anchor → v3.0 Part, 100% coverage), `kernel_v3_0_full_fold_outline.md` (103-subsection outline), `kernel_v3_0_full_fold_draft.md` (the kernel of record). The architectural migration is one-way: there is no v2.7.15 successor to v2.7.14; v3.0 is the continuation.
- **Four-stack attribution for v3.0:** Robin (architect, substrate phrasing, conversion-mode call, all verdicts), Claude / Anthropic (fold-map enumeration, outline assembly, full-fold splice, dual-reference refactor), Chris / ChatGPT (Phase 1.a discipline pass on §A–§G, standing audit correction weight; cleared the lived-practice qualifiers `structural` / `structural scope`), Muse Spark / Meta AI (external reviewer of record, not invoked for this task). Robin holds architectural continuity across model instances.

**Change from v2.7.13 to v2.7.14:**

The v2.7.14 change is the closing v2.7.x commit — six substantive additions to §9.7 plus one rename — and is the final v2.7.x version before the v3.0 restructuring. The §9.7 cluster now runs Def 9.7 → Lemma 9.7a → Def 9.7b → Lemma 9.7c → Rmk 9.7d → Rmk 9.7e → Rmk 9.7f → Rmk 9.7h → Note 9.7i → Rmk 9.7j → Rmk 9.7k → Rmk 9.7l → Note 9.7m → Note 9.7n. No new external authorities. No new Open Problems. Existing theorems unchanged.

- **Rename.** v2.7.13's Note 9.7g (Closure-lattice portability is conditional) → Note 9.7n, repositioned as the series-cap containment note after the v2.7.14 substantive additions; content unchanged, cross-references to Rmk 1.5b preserved. Scope / containment notes belong after the substantive content they constrain.
- **Rmk 9.7h** (CRT Fibered Presentation of the Multi-Prime Embedding; interpretive Garcia-bridge / unconditional polygonal-presentation fact). Under §9.7, $\mathrm{Div}(N) = \prod_i \mathrm{Div}(p_i^{a_i})$ is rendered as a Minkowski sum of prime-power fibers; the $i$-th prime contributes a regular $n_i$-bead polygonal fiber, $n_i = a_i + 1$, traced by exponent $e_i$. Coprime partition of the prime axes (Rmk 2.2e) gives fibered presentations. Canonical example: $\mathrm{Div}(720)$ renders as six translated pentagons. Geometric face of the CRT / product split already used by the harmonic register.
- **Note 9.7i** (Fiber-Level Non-Crystallographic Symmetry; interpretive analogy / unconditional independence-witness fact). Prime-power fibers may carry non-crystallographic polygonal symmetry even when the global phase skeleton is crystallographic. Two distinct axes govern fiber compatibility — local crystallographic status ($n_i \in \{1,2,3,4,6\}$) and global compatibility ($n_i \mid m$); at $m = 6$, global compatibility implies local but not conversely ($n_i = 4$ is locally crystallographic yet globally incompatible). Independence from $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$ is established by a 2×3 witness grid with all six cells populated. *"The pentagon and the strain are different witnesses. The square and the strain are different witnesses. The strain is neither."*
- **Rmk 9.7j** (Crystal-Graph Identification of Div(N); unconditional product-structure correspondence / interpretive Kashiwara framework-bridge). For $N = \prod p_i^{a_i}$, $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ carries the structure of the colored product crystal of $V_{a_1/2} \otimes \cdots \otimes V_{a_m/2}$ under the commuting action of $\mathfrak{sl}_2^{\oplus m}$; each prime contributes a length-$(a_i + 1)$ crystal chain and a pair of coefficient-free Kashiwara operators (multiplication = raising, division = lowering). The non-trivial Kashiwara tensor-product rule is *not* invoked — Div(N) sits at the commuting-actions case. Kashiwara is cited as a math-address for an existing structure, not as a source of inherited theorems. Four addresses for each finite-sector state: divisor, occupation vector, spin weight, crystal vertex. Cross-references Rmk 2.2a.1 — coefficient-free Kashiwara moves : weighted lifts :: Newton differences : Taylor coefficients.
- **Rmk 9.7k** (Local-Comb Cardinality Invariant; unconditional). The local comb at $d \in \mathrm{Div}(N)$ has cardinality $|\mathrm{comb}(d)| = 1 + \Omega_{\max}(N)$, constant across selection; the polygon-order multiset $\{a_i + 1\}$ is likewise selection-invariant.
- **Rmk 9.7l** (Superprimorial Polygon Signature; unconditional). For $\mathrm{Sp}_n = \prod_{i=1}^n p_i^{n-i+1}$, the staircase profile $(n, n-1, \ldots, 1)$ produces polygon-order multiset $\{2, 3, \ldots, n+1\}$ — a complete consecutive run, each polygon order once. For $\mathrm{Sp}_6$: $\tau = 5040 = 7!$, $\Omega_{\max} = 21$, multiset $\{2,3,4,5,6,7\}$. Precision pass (per Chris): the multiset is permutation-invariant — a *family classifier* identifying integers with exponent multiset $\{1, \ldots, n\}$ on $n$ primes, NOT $\mathrm{Sp}_n$ uniquely.
- **Note 9.7m** (Position-Awareness as Anti-Hallucination Property; interpretive). The structural facts of 9.7h / 9.7k / 9.7l together supply position-awareness — an agent at $d \in \mathrm{Div}(N)$ can verify its claimed location against three locally-checkable invariants (cardinality, polygon-order multiset, strand-position vector), at $O(m)$ cost. Framed against the Lucretian Canon (perception > prediction, transparency > authority, humility > certainty) as a structurally-local consistency check at the position layer, with explicit refusal of overclaim — it does not eliminate hallucination.
- §16 Key Theorems Reference: six new entries plus the 9.7g → 9.7n rename.
- **Discipline passes** (Chris). An exploratory draft reaching for "the §9.7 embedding is a cut-and-project construction" and "$\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$ measures how quasicrystalline the lattice is" was pulled back (cut-and-project requires an infinite higher-dimensional periodic lattice through an irrational window; §9.7 has a finite divisor poset, rational phasing, rational radii — resemblance, not mechanism). A Taylor / factorial exploratory thread was caught as redundant with Rmk 2.2a.1 and Note 2.2a.2. The Kashiwara framework-vs-theorems separation was enforced; an "9.7h / 9.7i as bridge theorems" mistyping was corrected to bridge remark / bridge note.
- Companion artifact: `helix_lattice.html`, updated to render the new readouts (crystal-graph strand renderer, local-comb cardinality panel, prime-colored polygon-order list, three-invariant position-awareness check).
- **Four-stack attribution for v2.7.14:** Robin (architectural origination — the "six pentagons" recognition under the helix_lattice top-down view triggering 9.7h, the staircase-signature insight for 9.7l, position-awareness as the Lucretian-Canon discipline target for 9.7m; the version-plan decision to make v2.7.14 the final v2.7.x and bundle all six additions), Claude / Anthropic (initial drafts of all six additions, cross-reference structure, the 2×3 grid for 9.7i, kernel splice, the 9.7g-rename resolution memo), Chris / ChatGPT (discipline passes — cut-and-project overclaim, $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$-quasicrystallinity claim, "characteristic" vs "exclusive" in 9.7l, bridge-remark/note typing, the Kashiwara framework-vs-theorems separation, the Taylor/Newton existing-kernel audit catch), Muse Spark / Meta AI (initial Div(720720) Ω=4 SVG slice, corrected via Claude audit; corrected Div(30030) Ω=4 SVG).

**Change from v2.7.11 to v2.7.13 (jump):**

*Note:* This entry records the v2.7.13 change. v2.7.12 was an intermediate working version folded into the v2.7.13 commit and carries no standalone changelog content (consistent with `SKILL.md` Version History, which has no v2.7.12 entry). The bibliography lands directly at v2.7.13.

The v2.7.13 change is the multi-prime divisor-lattice embedding pass — the entire §9.7 section committed as a single coherent block, plus Note 9.3a.1, a preamble note distinguishing the canonical concentric helix form from the multi-prime translated form. §16 Key Theorems Reference: nine new entries. No new external authorities. No new Open Problems. Existing theorems unchanged.

- **Def 9.7** (definitional): the multi-prime divisor-lattice embedding — a per-prime translated and rotated helix family with phases $\varphi_i = 2\pi i/m$, radii $r_i = 1/n_i$, height $z = \Omega(d) = \sum e_i$; the unit on the spine; cylinders share the spine-contact locus by construction. Lemma 9.3a's disjoint-cylinder regime is intentionally abandoned in this form, a trade marked by Note 9.3a.1 — the two forms are structurally complementary, not in conflict.
- **Lemma 9.7a** (unconditional): each Hasse cover edge $d \to d \cdot p_i$ is the translated one-step chord of prime $i$'s shifted helix; the Hasse diagram is the helix family read as a graph.
- **Def 9.7b** (definitional): the apex defect $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(N) = |A(N)|^2$, the squared $L^2$-norm of the first phase-weighted Fourier coefficient of the exponent-multiplicity profile $f(i) = (e^{-2\pi i/n_i} - 1)/n_i$ on $\mathbb{Z}/m$, equivalently $|m \cdot \widehat{f}(-1)|^2$, equivalently the squared distance of $\mathbf{P}(\mathrm{apex})$ from the spine — the Plancherel residual of multiplicity asymmetry under canonical prime phasing.
- **Lemma 9.7c** (unconditional): the cancellation law — $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(N) = 0$ exactly when $\widehat{f}(-1) = 0$; constant-multiplicity profiles $a_i \equiv k$ are the canonical structural family, closing by character orthogonality on $\mathbb{Z}/m$; squarefree is the $k = 1$ doorway. Seven verified examples via the helix-lattice viewer instrument: Div(30) / (900) / (1001) / (30030) all close at $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}} = 0$ (constant profiles, including non-squarefree $(2,2,2)$); Div(720) / (2520) / (720720) carry asymmetric residuals 0.893 / 1.141 / 1.864.
- **Rmk 9.7d** (interpretive): compressed-shadow framing. A pure-$T^3$ readout collapses the open $\Omega$-spine into cyclic closure; the February 2026 12.5% Hasse-residual term in chord-distance MDS over 22 tickers structured by Div(720) is read as a candidate apex-defect quantity under the wrong projection — diagnostic rather than theorem-level until the normalization between chord-distance residual and $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}$ is fixed (Chris's discipline-pass downgrade).
- **Rmk 9.7e** (interpretive): arithmetic-crystal vocabulary. $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ as a graded arithmetic crystal in $\mathbb{R}^3$ — constant multiplicity = closed crystal (geometric closure on the $\Omega$-spine), asymmetric multiplicity = strained crystal, $T^3$ projection = crystal shadow, open-$\Omega$ embedding = crystal with rank restored. $\|A(N)\|^2$ has the form of a dimensionless squared strain / energy-like defect; the mechanical vocabulary is structurally faithful but is not a claim of physical elasticity — no units, no constitutive law, no stiffness tensor (Chris's discipline-pass softening).
- **Rmk 9.7f** (interpretive structural identity, unconditional poset-product fact): basis/complement Boolean cube congruence and joined objects. Two integers with identical multiplicity profile and identical $m$ embed as identical §9.7 crystals; $\mathrm{Div}(30)$ and $\mathrm{Div}(1001)$ are congruent Boolean cubes $P_2 \square P_2 \square P_2$ — the basis cube and the complement cube of the FVLT 720/1001 lens architecture. Coprime joins: $\mathrm{Div}(N_1 N_2) \cong \mathrm{Div}(N_1) \times \mathrm{Div}(N_2)$, embedded as a Minkowski sum of reframed sub-figures. Strain non-additivity: $\delta_{\mathrm{apex}}(720720) = 1.864 \neq 0.893 + 0$ — constant-profile cancellation is $m$-relative, and the join contributes phase-redistribution strain that neither side carries alone.
- **Note 9.7g** (structural scope; renamed Note 9.7n at v2.7.14): closure-lattice portability is conditional. §9.7's theorems are unconditional inside the arithmetic divisor lattice and conditional outside it; a closure-lattice abstraction via FCA gives an architectural template, not an automatic theorem schema — rank, covers, phase/bank assignment, cancellation, strain, and join behavior must each be re-earned from the extracted substrate. Ontology joins must recompute strain globally. L1c (banked attention over ontology lattices) is an active research track, not a kernel theorem; the smallest real test is FCA on Serena's ArcadeDB tissue.
- **Three-stack attribution for v2.7.13:** Robin (architectural correction — constant multiplicity, not squarefree, as the canonical closure family; the arithmetic-crystal vocabulary; the basis/complement crystal congruence and joined-object framing; the closure-lattice portability scope and L1c containment), Chris / ChatGPT (discipline pass on §9.7 — tangent-generator cylinder language, translated-chord Hasse–helix language, exact-vs-canonical separation in the cancellation law; the closure-lattice / FCA transfer document; the epistemic-hygiene pass — February-residual downgrade, strain-energy softening, "squareful" → "integers" in the Boolean cube congruence, crystallographic-restriction note scoped to the unlabeled phase skeleton), Claude / Anthropic (instrument — `apexDefect` and the Div(900) preset in `helix_lattice.html`, smoke tests confirming all seven verified examples; the strain-non-additivity numerical verification at $m = 6$; three sharpenings on ontology transfer; the kernel splice across the §9.7 commit).

**Change from v2.7.10 to v2.7.11:**

The v2.7.11 change is a same-divisor-two-readouts pass: a single new interpretive remark (Rmk 1.5c) inserted immediately after Rmk 1.5b in §1.5 of the deep kernel. No new theorem, no new arithmetic claim, no new notation, no new external authority, no new Open Problems. This is architectural housekeeping that extends the v2.7.10 substrate-level pass.

The single entry is:

- New **Remark 1.5c** (Same Divisor, Two Readouts) inserted immediately after Rmk 1.5b in §1.5 Walk-Relative Lens. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive, depends: 1.5b / 5.5 / 8.19. For each ambient $N$, the same divisor $q \mid N$ participates in two readout regimes. *Hasse regime*: $q$ is a coordinate node of $\mathrm{Div}(N)$; navigational role — position in the prime-exponent lattice, Hasse-cover adjacencies, rank $\Omega(q)$, lens-relative relations to the unit $1$ and apex $N$. *Harmonic regime*: $q$ is a supercharacter / Ramanujan channel; measurement role — $q$ indexes the orbit-summed cyclic readout $c_q$ over $\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z}$ or over the relevant quotient/lens specified in §5. **Load-bearing pair**: *"the divisor $q$ is not merely a containment object and not merely a frequency object. It is a shared channel: its Hasse position says where the channel lives, its supercharacter readout says what the channel measures."* The two readouts are compatible with coprime factorization: in the fused lens $N_{\text{FUSED}} = 720 \cdot 1001$, the fibered coordinate presentation $\mathrm{Div}(720720) \cong \mathrm{Div}(720) \times \mathrm{Div}(1001)$ (Rmk 2.2e, v2.7.10) and the CRT factorization of Ramanujan channels $c_{720720}(s) = c_{720}(s) \cdot c_{1001}(s)$ (Theorem 8.19) express the same basis–complement separation in the coordinate and harmonic registers respectively.

**Scope discipline.** The remark introduces no new arithmetic claim; it records the architectural pairing between the coordinate material of §1 and the harmonic material of §5. The final scope-protective sentence — *"This remark introduces no new arithmetic claim; it records the architectural pairing between the coordinate material of §1 and the harmonic material of §5"* — is preserved verbatim from Robin's draft. The Genesis-layer claim — that the basis is not merely declared by prime axes but recoverable through cyclic orbit structure of $(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^\times$ — is explicitly out of scope for Rmk 1.5c and reserved for a later Genesis pass (deferred to v2.8 or a new Genesis remark/theorem in the Genesis pass). Rmk 1.5c carries only the two-readout pairing claim.

**Parallel with v2.7.10 Rmk 1.5b.** Both remarks name "two views over the same primitives" but along different axes. Rmk 1.5b: navigation substrate ($\mathrm{Div}(N)$) vs closure-of-description substrate ($\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$); same primitives, different question classes. Rmk 1.5c: containment readout (Hasse) vs harmonic readout (supercharacter); same divisor channels, different measurement modes. Both are substrate-level architectural framing; together they articulate the §1.5 architectural pass as Coordinate Substrate + Two Readouts. The two readouts are both anchored on the coordinate substrate of Rmk 1.5b.

- §16 Key Theorems Reference: one new entry (1.5c).
- Notation: no changes (uses existing $c_q$, $\Omega(q)$, $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ symbols).
- References: no changes.
- SKILL.md companion: section "Closure-of-Description Substrate (v2.7.10)" renamed to "Substrate-Level Architecture (v2.7.10–v2.7.11)" to accommodate the broader substrate-level framing; opener updated to identify both substrate distinction (Rmk 1.5b) and two-readout pairing (Rmk 1.5c) as architectural pillars; Quick Reference, File Routing, and Key Identities each gain one row for Rmk 1.5c.

**Provenance.** A document arrived after the v2.7.10 patch was shipped (within the same session-day, May 7, 2026), mapping the supercharacter work back onto the Hasse diagram architecture and proposing kernel placement. The document's two boxed shorter forms — *"Hasse diagram = where the divisor channel lives"* and *"Supercharacter = what that divisor channel measures"* — are the load-bearing summary sentences. Robin's audit identified that these belong as a substrate-level remark next to Rmk 1.5b, not as part of the deferred Genesis pass: the two-readout claim is needed before Genesis as the bridge between §1 (coordinate / Hasse) and §5 (harmonic / Ramanujan). Robin's draft of the kernel-safe wording included the explicit no-overclaim guard sentence; preserved verbatim in the final remark text. The Genesis-layer pass — adding a remark or theorem about basis self-construction from the orbit structure of $(\mathbb{Z}/N\mathbb{Z})^\times$ — is reserved for a later session.

**Three-stack attribution for v2.7.11:**
- **Robin** (architectural call: integrate now as Rmk 1.5c, leave Genesis pass for later; *"this is the same kind of architectural clarification as Rmk 1.5b — names a substrate-level pairing already implicit across the kernel"*; specified placement immediately after Rmk 1.5b; drafted the kernel-safe wording; enforced no-overclaim discipline by reserving basis-self-construction for a later remark/theorem in the Genesis pass; *"do not overload 1.5c with that yet"*).
- **Chris / ChatGPT / OpenAI** (drafted the original two-readout document arriving after v2.7.10; provided the Hasse-as-where / supercharacter-as-what shorter formulation; identified the Genesis-layer connection but kept it explicit and separable rather than folded in).
- **Claude / Anthropic** (audited the document against existing kernel content inventory — Hasse diagram in §1.4 / §2, Ramanujan channels in §5, fibered split in Rmk 2.2e, CRT factorization in Thm 8.19, supercharacters in [FGK14]; identified parallel with Rmk 1.5b structure; proposed three placement options 1.5c / 5.7b / Genesis pass; executed integration under Robin's call).

**Change from v2.7.9 to v2.7.10:**

The v2.7.10 change is a closure-of-description substrate pass: four new entries that name a second lattice over the trainer's primitives ($\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$, the closure-of-description substrate via Galois connection on derivation operators) and the operational architecture under which trainer-derived attributes accumulate. One new external authority ([Bir40]); no new Open Problems; no theorem changes; one renumbering pass during kernel integration.

The four entries are:

- New **Remark 1.5b** (Coordinate Substrate vs. Closure-of-Description Substrate) inserted after Rmk 1.4e at the end of §1.5 Walk-Relative Lens. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Names two lattices that arise over the trainer's primitives. The *coordinate substrate* $\text{Div}(N)$ has nodes as integer divisors, edges as single-prime Hasse covers, geometry from prime-exponent coordinates; it answers navigation questions. The *closure-of-description substrate* $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}(O, \Sigma, I)$ arises whenever attributes are tracked over the trainer's primitive objects: given object set $O$, attribute set $\Sigma$, and incidence $I \subseteq O \times \Sigma$, the derivation operators $A' = \{\sigma \in \Sigma : \forall o \in A,\; oI\sigma\}$ and $B' = \{o \in O : \forall \sigma \in B,\; oI\sigma\}$ form a contravariant adjunction (Galois connection) between $(\mathcal{P}(O), \subseteq)$ and $(\mathcal{P}(\Sigma), \subseteq)$; closed pairs $(A, B)$ with $A' = B$ and $B' = A$ ordered by extent inclusion form a complete lattice $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ that answers closure questions. **Load-bearing sentence:** *"the two lattices share the same primitive objects but answer different questions."* Canonical case ($\Sigma$ = prime-power divisibility predicates over the join-irreducibles of $\text{Div}(N)$) recovers $\text{Div}(N)$ up to isomorphism via the order-ideal representation theorem [Bir40]; observation-derived attributes (walker history, lens state, atom/jump/zero classification of Rmk 9.3f, domain class of Def 2.2c filtered by visit context) cause $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ to diverge from $\text{Div}(N)$ and provide genuinely new structure. The trainer renders $\text{Div}(N)$ as 3D geometry and reserves $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ as a semantic overlay over accumulated observations (§1.6).

- New **Remark 2.2e** (Fibered Presentation of Coprime-Decomposable Lenses) inserted after Def 2.2d in §2.1 Factorial Shells. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. For $N = A \cdot B$ with $\gcd(A, B) = 1$, every divisor $n \mid N$ factors uniquely as $n = a \cdot b$ with $a \mid A$, $b \mid B$ by unique prime factorization. The map $\varphi: \text{Div}(N) \to \text{Div}(A) \times \text{Div}(B)$, $n \mapsto (\gcd(n, A), \gcd(n, B))$ is an order isomorphism with inverse $(a, b) \mapsto a \cdot b$. The divisor lattice decomposes as the *Cartesian product* of partial orders (not a box product, per Chris's terminology correction) with the corresponding Cartesian graph product on Hasse diagrams: cover edges inherited from factors, no mixed-direction covers. Canonical FVLT instance: $N_{\text{FUSED}} = N_{\text{BASIS}} \cdot N_{\text{COMPLEMENT}} = 720 \cdot 1001 = 720720$ giving $\text{Div}(720720) \cong \text{Div}(720) \times \text{Div}(1001)$ with $|\text{Div}(720720)| = 30 \cdot 8 = 240$ nodes (cf. Thm 8.19 for the Ramanujan-sum analogue). Trainer's fibered embedding renders this directly: each $n = b \cdot c$ at world coordinates from basis exponents $(e_2, e_3, e_5)$ within a basis-cell, the cell indexed by complement exponents $(e_7, e_{11}, e_{13})$ across a $2 \times 2 \times 2$ super-arrangement; every divisor of $N_{\text{FUSED}}$ occupies a unique geometric position with no projection from higher dimension. General statement extends to any pairwise coprime decomposition $N = \prod_i N_i$.

- New **§1.6 Observation Architecture** subsection inserted between §1.5 Walk-Relative Lens and §2 The Factorial Lattice. Two remarks formalize the trainer's observation primitives.

- New **Remark 1.6a** (Factorization Regime) at start of §1.6. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. Three operationally distinct factorization regimes: *cold* (factor new ambient $N$ from scratch, $O(\sqrt N)$ trial division for direct rendering range; the trainer does not assume cold factorization is available for large unstructured $N$), *warm* (within known lens $\text{Div}(N)$ with prime support $\pi(N)$, divisibility against $\pi(N)$ only, $O(r \cdot \log m)$), *walk-relative* (atom step $n \mapsto n \cdot p^\varepsilon$ updates one exponent coordinate, $O(1)$). Trainer's operational cost is dominated by warm + walk-relative; cold is invoked only at lattice construction or when the user supplies a target outside the current ambient's prime support. Direct-rendering cap on $N$ is a *node-count* cap $\prod_i (a_i + 1) \leq N_{\text{render}}$ — NOT a *factorization-cost* cap. Lattices with structurally tractable construction sequences (factorial, lcm-of-range, axis-add expansions) reach values far past direct-rendering cap without ever invoking cold factorization. Regime distinction is structural, not algorithmic. Chris's audit softened the cold-factorization complexity sentence to remove broad large-$N$ algorithm claims; the trial-division statement is scoped to the direct-rendering range.

- New **Remark 1.6b** (Three Observation Contexts) at end of §1.6. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Three distinct levels of primitive object support trainer-derived attributes; each level supports its own $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ (Rmk 1.5b); conflating the levels yields invalid closure structure. *Node context*: $O_{\text{node}} = \text{Div}(N)$, intrinsic structural properties (domain class, $\omega(n)$, $\Omega(n)$, pairCoords, lensStatus); persistent across walks. *Visit context*: $O_{\text{visit}} = $ ordered pairs $(k, n_k)$ from a trajectory, per-event properties (was-target, was-revisit, lens-state-at-visit, $k$th-revisit-of-this-value); specific to single trajectory. *Transition context*: $O_{\text{trans}} = $ ordered triples $(k, n_k, n_{k+1})$, per-move properties (layer in {atom, jump, zero}, prime-used, polynomial expression, sign-flip); specific to single trajectory. The three contexts answer three different questions: structural classes of nodes (intrinsic), kinds of visit-events (per-trajectory), kinds of moves (per-trajectory). Cross-context queries require constructing a fourth context with attributes pulled across levels, not merging existing ones. Trainer's data structures preserve separation: LatticeNode (node-context), WalkerVisit (visit-context), WalkerTransition (transition-context). The reserved attributes slots in the v0.2 walker schema (WalkerVisit.attributes, WalkerTransition.attributes) are the kernel-grounded entry point for $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ overlay computation in subsequent trainer versions.

- §16 Key Theorems Reference: four new entries added (1.5b, 2.2e, 1.6a, 1.6b) — first §1.x entries in the reference table.
- Notation: $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}(O, \Sigma, I)$ added (closure-of-description substrate from Galois connection on incidence); $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}(O_{\text{node}}), \Lambda_{\text{desc}}(O_{\text{visit}}), \Lambda_{\text{desc}}(O_{\text{trans}})$ for the three contexts of Rmk 1.6b.
- References: [Bir40] (Birkhoff, *Lattice Theory*, 1940) added to Algebra and Representation Theory section; cited from Rmk 1.5b and Rmk 1.6b. Order-ideal representation theorem for finite distributive lattices is the load-bearing reference for the canonical-$\Sigma$ recovery $\text{Div}(N) \cong \Lambda_{\text{desc}}$. No FCA citations in the kernel; community positioning reserved for paper. The Galois lineage (Galois 1830s → Birkhoff 1940 *Lattice Theory* → Ore 1944 abstract Galois connections → Wille 1982 restructuring → Ganter & Wille 1999 *Formal Concept Analysis* → FVLT 2026) is documented in the Last-updated header but not internalized into the kernel as cited authority beyond [Bir40].
- Companion artifact: **3D Universal Lattice Trainer v0.1.3** (Robin + Claude, May 6–7 2026) — universal divisor-lattice trainer with FVLT canonical lenses as privileged presets. Files: `lattice_scene_generator.ts` (1727 lines, 118 tests green), `lattice_viewer_scaffold.jsx` (1589 lines, 19 universal-N tests + 9 patch tests green). Universal Div(N) input front door with factorization parser ("720720", "2^4·3^2·5·7·11·13", whitespace-separated factor lists all accepted); external-prime guard; conditional FVLT panels; UI label SKELETON for canonical FVLT, SPLIT for generic two-cube; sceneSlug for scene-correct export filenames; cube outlines toggle (8 wireframe boxes for fibered, 2 for two-cube, 1 for cartesian); PNG/JSON export panel. Fibered mode default for $\text{Div}(720720)$ renders $G_{720720} \cong G_{720} \square G_{1001}$ directly with formula world = SUPER_SPACING·complementExp + BASIS_SCALE·basisExp; eight basis-cells in 2×2×2 complement super-cube. Walker schema for v0.2 (Chris-locked, ready to implement) includes WalkerVisit.attributes and WalkerTransition.attributes slots reserved for $\Lambda_{\text{desc}}$ overlay (slots only, no computation in v0.2; Lambda_desc overlay deferred to v0.4+). Walker scope for v0.2: positive divisor lattice base, signed walker at $|x|$, sign as decoration color, zero as separate hub off-lattice; lens auto-rebuilds to Div(lcm($|$start$|$, $|$end$|$)); trajectory polyline color-coded by atom (orange) / jump (purple) / zero (teal-grey). Bounds: $|x| \leq 10^8$, lcm $\leq 5 \times 10^6$.
- **Numbering note (renumbering pass during deep-kernel integration).** Remarks were drafted as Rmk 3.5b (fibered presentation), Rmk 9.4a (factorization regime), Rmk 9.4d (three observation contexts) against the SKILL.md summary. Deep-kernel integration surfaced two collisions: (a) §3 in the kernel is "Trilattice and Möbius Partition" (residue symmetry, gearbox, calculus convergence), conceptually unrelated to coprime-decomposition fibered presentation — the natural home in the kernel is §2.1 Factorial Shells, where the lattice-arithmetic letter sequence (Def 2.2b lattice metric, Cor 2.2b.1 coprime distance, Prop 2.2b.2 divisor lattice intersection, Def 2.2c domain classification, Def 2.2d shell function) continues naturally as Rmk 2.2e fibered presentation; (b) kernel §9.4 "Connections to Classical Structures" already contains Rmk 9.4a (Walk Regime Taxonomy: Helix / Circle / Spiral) and Note 9.4b (Farey Boundary Hypothesis) — direct collision. New content placed in §1.6 Observation Architecture as a real subsection between §1.5 Walk-Relative Lens and §2 The Factorial Lattice. The §1.6 placement is the right semantic home: instrument mechanics (factorization cost, observation contexts) live alongside the Hasse Instrument (§1.4) and Walk-Relative Lens (§1.5) in §1, not in §9 (Farey Spine Realization) which is geometric realization rather than instrument architecture. Renaming committed: 3.5b → 2.2e; 9.4a → 1.6a; 9.4d → 1.6b. Renumbering applied uniformly to deep kernel, SKILL.md summary, and bibliography.

**Three-stack attribution for v2.7.10:**
- **Robin** (architectural insight that FVLT is preset overlay rather than root behavior; kernel-content directive "I don't really care what the mathematicians think — codify what applies to the kernel"; FCA textbook upload — Ganter & Wille 1999, *Formal Concept Analysis: Mathematical Foundations*, Springer 1999; final accept on the four remarks).

**Change from v2.7.8 to v2.7.9:**

The v2.7.9 change is the substrate-layer fraction-boundary pass: three interpretive entries clarifying the difference/slope register distinction and the unit floor. No theorem changes, no new mathematical content, no new external authorities, no new Open Problems. Surfaced through Universal Lattice Trainer v2 / v2.13 development (Robin + Claude, May 5 2026); audited and refined by Chris/ChatGPT.

- New **Remark 2.2a.1** (Difference–Slope Duality) inserted in §2.1. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Forward differences live in the *difference register* where no denominator has yet been introduced; standard polynomial coefficients pick up factorial denominators when the same discrete motion is translated into the *slope/calculus register*. Binomial basis $\binom{x}{k}$ makes the toll explicit (falling factorial $(x)_k$ as numerator, $k!$ as denominator); factorial filtration $N_k = k!$ measures the basis-change cost between the fraction-free lattice register and the slope-normalized calculus register. **Load-bearing sentence:** *"the denominator is not missing from the lattice register; it has not yet been introduced."* **Guardrail (carrier-usage independence):** factorial denominator is a basis-change cost, NOT a register-crossing diagnostic; carrier usage is detected by the atom/jump/zero typology of Rmk 9.3f (v2.7.8), not by the standard-form denominator. Two worked counterexamples settle this: pure-Hasse-atomic walk $[12, 4, 2, 1]$ has standard form $(1/6)(-5x^3 + 33x^2 - 76x + 72)$ — denominator 6 despite zero carrier crossings; $[6, 12, 30, 60]$ contains a non-atomic jump (12→30, ratio 5/2) yet standardizes to $6x^2 + 6$ — denominator 1 via algebraic cancellation.

- New **Note 2.2a.2** (Lattice Metric ↔ Polynomial Degree, Descent-through-Zero Walks) inserted after Rmk 2.2a.1 in §2.1. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. For walks $n \to \text{atom}^* 1 \to \text{zero } 0 \to \text{jump } m$ where atomic descent is a monotone shortest path through the Hasse diagram, visit count is $\Omega(n) + 3$, Newton interpolant has degree at most $\Omega(n) + 2$, and standard-form denominator divides $(\Omega(n) + 2)!$. Identifies the lattice metric $d(n, 1) = \Omega(n)$ with the polynomial signature degree of descent-through-zero walks (modulo +2 offset). Two senses of *shell* distinguished: lattice shell ($\mathrm{Div}(720)$ hosting nodes up to $\Omega = 7$) vs polynomial shell (factorial filtration rung $N_k = k!$ that the descent-through-zero walk's signature requires to standardize).

- New **Remark 9.3g** (The Unit Floor and Fraction Boundary) inserted in §9.3a. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Names $1$ as the floor of multiplicative descent in two senses simultaneously — *multiplicative identity* (root of every $\mathrm{Div}(N)$, terminus of any monotone $\div p$ Hasse descent) and *register boundary* (lower bound of integer divisor lattice; below 1, continued division produces fractions, exiting $\mathrm{Div}(N)$ into the slope register). Together with Rmk 9.3e zero clause (v2.7.7) and Rmk 9.3f atom/jump/zero typology (v2.7.8) this completes the substrate-layer fraction-boundary architecture.

- **§16 Key Theorems Reference (v2.7.9 only):** three new entries (2.2a.1, 2.2a.2, 9.3g) — first §9.x entries in the reference table.

- Companion artifact: **Universal Lattice Trainer v2.13** (Robin, May 2026) implementing Newton Signature v1 — measurement-only readout of an already-produced walk that fits the minimum-degree Newton interpolant. Trainer's `newtonSignature` function returns the standardized-form polynomial; trainer does not interpret denominators as carrier-event diagnostics (per the Rmk 2.2a.1 guardrail).

**Three-stack attribution for v2.7.9:**

- **Claude / Anthropic** (drafted the three remarks under Robin's spec; produced the two worked counterexamples settling the carrier-usage independence; kernel splice in §2.1 and §9.3a; §16 Key Theorems Reference entries).
- **Chris / ChatGPT / OpenAI** (discipline pass — confirmed the difference vs slope basis-change framing; required two-counterexamples pattern; confirmed unconditional vs interpretive status assignments).

**Change from v2.7.7 to v2.7.8:**

The v2.7.8 change is a single new definitional remark in §9.3 — Rmk 9.3f (Operational Transition Typology on the Signed Substrate). No theorem changes, no new mathematical content, no new external authorities, no new Open Problems. Surfaced through Universal Lattice Trainer v2 development; audited by Chris/ChatGPT.

- New **Remark 9.3f** (Operational Transition Typology on the Signed Substrate) inserted in §9.3a. **Status: definitional**, register: definitional. Distinguishes three operational transition types in trainer walks over $\mathbb{Z}$:
  - *atom* — sign-preserving Hasse cover, single prime factor difference, ratio $p^{\pm 1}$;
  - *jump* — polynomial-expression-required transition between nonzero values (any non-atomic transition that can be standardized as a Newton interpolant);
  - *zero* — carrier event with $n = 0$ or $n' = 0$ (transition through the additive carrier hub of Rmk 9.3e).

  Test order is *zero first, atom second, jump residual* — every transition that isn't a zero-event is checked for atom; non-atom non-zero is jump by elimination. **Keystone guardrail:** "Operational" in the title is load-bearing — the remark is implementation vocabulary made explicit, NOT a mathematical partition theorem about all maps on $\mathbb{Z}$. Sign-changing targets may be routed via $\pm 1 \to 0 \to m'$ when the instrument elects to separate multiplicative descent from additive launch; direct AP sign-changing jumps remain valid carrier jumps when explicitly declared.

- **§16 Key Theorems Reference:** new entry for 9.3f added (deferred to consolidated entry-add at v2.7.9).

- Companion artifact: **Universal Lattice Trainer v2.x** (Robin, May 2026) implementing the typology in `classifyTransition(n, n')` — atom/jump/zero classification colors trajectory polyline (orange / purple / teal-grey).
