**Change from v2.7.5 to v2.7.6:**

The v2.7.6 change is a single interpretive remark added to §9.3a — Rmk 9.3d (Architectural Content of the Residue Fan). No theorem changes, no new mathematical content, no new external authorities, no new Open Problems. The remark names a selection rule that was implicit in the conjunction of §3 (residue symmetry), §8 (basis/complement), and §9.1a (rational skeleton + exponential lift) but had not been articulated as a single criterion: when does the residue-class affine fan $y = Nx + b$ read as basis-versus-complement architecture rather than mere modular arithmetic?

The selection rule has two conjoint conditions:
- **(i) Multi-prime basis**: $|B(N)| \geq 2$, where $B(N) = \mathrm{supp}(N)$ is the prime support of $N$. Without this, the non-unit sector of the fan is a single-prime tower (e.g., $N = 8 = 2^3$ gives a 2-primary central column with no internal basis interaction to collapse against), and the basis/complement architectural reading of Definition 8.12 does not activate.
- **(ii) Complement-supported $b = 1$ opening**: $N + 1$ supported on $B'(N) = \mathrm{primes} \setminus B(N)$. This is automatic from $\gcd(N, N+1) = 1$, but the *strength* of the opening depends on what $N + 1$ actually is — a single complement prime, a complement-product, or a mixed support that includes far-complement primes.

Minimal small-modulus examples are $N \in \{6, 10, 12\}$ with single-prime complement openings ($6+1 = 7$, $10+1 = 11$, $12+1 = 13$). The factorial shell $N_0 = 720$ is the strengthened form: by Theorem 3.7 (residue symmetry), $719 \equiv -1 \pmod{d}$ and $721 \equiv +1 \pmod{d}$ for every $d \mid 720$ with $d > 1$, while $721 = 7 \cdot 103$ is complement-supported and includes the canonical near-complement prime $7 \in B' = \{7, 11, 13\}$. The architectural reading is therefore uniform across the entire 30-element divisor lattice of 720, not just over the four arms of a small-N fan.

The remark connects condition (i) to Theorem 8.20h.3: the geometric prerequisite $|B| \geq 2$ activates the basis collapse; the topological prerequisite $r \geq 3$ further requires the proper-complement order complex $\Delta(B_r^\circ)$ to carry a nontrivial 1-cycle; both prerequisites hold at $N_0 = 720$. The condition-(i)-only regime ($|B| = 2$, e.g., $N \in \{6, 10, 12\}$) has the basis collapse but not the loop topology; the factorial shell at $S_6$ has both. This is what the v2.6 closure work (Theorem 8.20h.3) refines beyond what the 2018-19 hand-drawn sheet was already selecting on.

**Annotation:** `<!-- id: RMK-9.3d-RESIDUE-FAN-ARCHITECTURE | register: interpretive | status: interpretive | provenance: original | depends: 3.7, 8.12, 8.20h.3, 9.1a, 9.3a -->`. Status note: "Interpretive. The arithmetic claims (residue identity for $N + 1$ on the $b = 1$ ray, Theorem 3.7 uniformity at $N_0 = 720$, basis/complement support of 721) are unconditional. The 'architectural content' reading is interpretive: it identifies a structural regime under which the residue fan reads as basis-versus-complement architecture in the sense of Definition 8.12, but the underlying affine geometry exists for any $N$."

**Provenance.** The visualization of residue classes as rays from a center belongs to the classical modular-arithmetic and arithmetic-progression tradition. The primitive arms ($\gcd(b, N) = 1$) are the reduced residue classes modulo $N$, i.e. the arithmetic progressions appearing in Dirichlet's theorem on primes in APs. The FVLT contribution is not the visual scaffolding but the architectural reading: the basis/complement split, the K(N) coprime spectrum as a structural object, and the conjunction-(i)+(ii) selection rule under which the classical fan reads as basis/complement architecture. Robin's 2018-19 hand-drawn residue-class ray diagram (mod 12, with explicit equation $y = \mathrm{mod} \cdot x + b$ rendered as residue rays from a center, prime/composite labels per ray, basis collapse in the central region, complement primes on the b = {1, 5, 7, 11} arms) records her working-through of the inherited classical scaffolding on the way to the FVLT architectural reading — not the origin of that scaffolding. Surfaced in the conversation immediately following the v2.7.5 patch session — Robin uploaded the diagram with the gloss "1 to 11 mod 10. 1 to 13 mod 12. 1 to 7 mod 6," and the conversation worked through what the implicit selection rule was. The Cartesian-fan progression as Robin's working notes documented: 2018-19 hand-drawn (working sheet, residue rays as classical scaffolding, prime/composite labels per ray) → 2019 GeoGebra under hypatiaa handle (formalizing the rays for mod 12 specifically) → 2022 cartesia2 (complement prime 7 identified as natural denominator in the Farey spine — the first FVLT-specific reading) → 2024 GeoGebra "Factorial Oracle" (gearbox formula $k_1 = N - 1$, $k_2 = N + 1$ — trilattice in pre-formal form) → kernel v2.6.1 §9.1a / Lemma 9.3a (formal helix family + no-intersection lemma) → kernel v2.7.4 Rmk 9.3c (substrate-level $\varphi$ on $\Gamma_5$) → kernel v2.7.6 Rmk 9.3d (this addition: selection rule for architectural content).

**Internal refinement (provenance/naming pass), within v2.7.6:** Robin flagged two precise corrections after the initial v2.7.6 commit — "the ancestor is older than me; the residue classes are also dirichlet arithmetic progressions" — leading to a sub-patch within v2.7.6 that (a) added the classical-object name to Rmk 9.3d's opening (residue classes y = Nx + b are an affine fan whose primitive arms are the reduced residue classes modulo $N$, i.e. the arithmetic progressions appearing in Dirichlet's theorem on primes in APs), (b) added an explicit "this affine fan is classical; the FVLT-specific content is the selection rule" sentence to thread the classical-vs-FVLT distinction, and (c) reframed the closing paragraph and provenance language so the residue-ray scaffolding is identified as classical and the 2018-19 hand-drawn sheet is described as Robin's working-through of inherited scaffolding rather than the origin of the visual structure. Brief packaged for Chris/ChatGPT audit before applying; cleared by Chris with two specific wording adjustments (use "reduced residue classes modulo $N$" as the noun rather than "Dirichlet arithmetic progressions" — Dirichlet's name attaches to the theorem about primes in APs, not to the residue-class object itself; do not add [Dir1837] citation since the theorem is not load-bearing for the FVLT selection rule) and confirmation that no version bump was needed (no theorem change, no annotation register change, no §16 change). Refinement applied.

**Change from v2.7.4 to v2.7.5:**

The v2.7.5 changes are an architectural-disambiguation patch to §8.4a: no new mathematical content, no new external authorities, no new open problems. The patch resolves an internal contradiction identified by post-v2.7.4 audit between Theorem 8.20a's bridge-pair list (subset-position pairing) and Remark 8.20g.2's totient-correspondence framing. Both were presented as "the eight §8.4a bridge pairs," both marked unconditional, but they specified different eight-element pair sets (basis-side overlap: {1, 6, 10}). The contradiction blocked unambiguous downstream measurement: H_bridge (Rmk 5.5c), ce_z (Def 8.20f), and β (Def 8.20b) were all defined per "the eight bridge pairs" without specifying which family. Originated from a session in which Claude audited the FVLT program suite against the kernel and found that the Hasse Tower's hard-coded BRIDGES array was structurally divergent from Rmk 8.20g.2's stated convention; deeper audit revealed that the divergence was inside the kernel itself (between Thm 8.20a and Rmk 8.20g.2), not between kernel and program. Robin's architectural decision: keep both structures, rename, declare canonical assignment, preserve the totient insight without rewriting what β measures.

- **Theorem 8.20a — bridge family rename and canonical assignment.** The Br_k notation throughout 8.20a renamed to $\mathrm{Br}_k^{\mathrm{pos}}$ (subset-position) for k ∈ {1, 2, 3}. The explicit Br₃ list — $\{(1,1), (2,7), (3,11), (5,13), (6,77), (10,91), (15,143), (30,1001)\}$ — preserved unchanged; this is the family the Hasse Tower has always implemented. Added explicit canonical-assignment statement: "$\mathrm{Br}_k^{\mathrm{pos}}$ is the canonical bridge family for the §8.4a internal bridge filtration, the bridge depth β(x) (Definition 8.20b), the complement energy Z-score ce_z (Definition 8.20f), and the bridge concentration index H_bridge (Remark 5.5c)."
- **Remark 8.20g.2 — rewrite from "instead" framing to parallel-layer framing.** Removed language reading the eight §8.4a bridge objects as "instead the levelwise totient correspondences." Introduced $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\varphi} = \{(c, \varphi(c)) : c \in \text{Div}(1001)\} = \{(1,1), (7,6), (11,10), (13,12), (77,60), (91,72), (143,120), (1001,720)\}$ as a parallel correspondence family supplementing $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$. Added Comparison subsection stating the basis-side element sets explicitly ($\{1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 10, 15, 30\}$ vs. $\{1, 6, 10, 12, 60, 72, 120, 720\}$, overlap $\{1, 6, 10\}$). Both families share cardinality (8), cross-domain support, and metric-free property (Δ = 0 under Cor 8.20g) but differ on six of eight pairs. Retained the isometric-embedding result for $G_{\mathrm{Exp}} \hookrightarrow G_{720720}$ (which holds independently of which bridge family is in use). Adjusted depends list to include 8.20a. Added "Canonical assignment" subsection stating explicitly that the §8.4a internal bridge filtration, β, ce_z, and H_bridge are defined against $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$, with $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\varphi}$ available as an additional structure for analyses that probe the closure identity directly but not the indexing set for the canonical diagnostics.
- **Terminology pass — "bridge edges" reservation enforced** per Rmk 8.20g.2's existing rule reserving the term for the three atomic Hasse covers $1 \to 7$, $1 \to 11$, $1 \to 13$ (the bridge interface). Patched seven occurrences across the kernel: Rmk 5.5c (4 occurrences — definition body, $H_{\mathrm{bridge}}$ definition, σ = 3/4 paragraph, status note); Rmk 8.20h.2 (2 occurrences — "bridge edges between Div(24) and Div(35)" and same for Div(720)/Div(1001)); notation glossary (1 occurrence — e_i entry). After the pass, only two "bridge edges" usages remain in the kernel, both correct: the canonical reservation rule itself in Rmk 8.20g.2, and the v2.7.5 changelog comment block at the top of the kernel quoting the rule.
- **Cross-reference correction in Rmk 5.5c.** The previous citation "(Thm 8.14)" for the eight bridge objects pointed at the Totient–Factorial Identity, which establishes φ(7·11·13) = 720 but does not enumerate eight bridge objects. Corrected to "(Theorem 8.20a, $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$)". The H_bridge definition body and status note also updated to reference the canonical family explicitly.
- **Metadata: Rmk 5.5c annotation status alignment.** The annotation header read `register: remark | status: empirical` while the inline status note read "*Status: Draft.*" Aligned to `register: remark | status: draft (empirical)` with depends list updated from "thm-8-14" to "thm-8-20a".
- **Metadata: Theorem 12.5 dependency tightened.** Annotation read `depends: 12.3, 12.6` but Rmk 12.6 itself depends on Thm 12.5 (per its own annotation); the circular reference is structurally invalid. Corrected to `depends: 12.2, 12.3` — the actual logical premises are the sumset obstruction (Thm 12.2) and the abelian quotient (Cor 12.3); Rmk 12.6 is downstream interpretive content, not a premise.

New entry for Rmk 8.20g.2 added (it was absent from §16 entirely): "Bridge correspondence layers: $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\mathrm{pos}}$ (canonical) and parallel totient-image $\mathrm{Br}_3^{\varphi}$ are metrically free, not Hasse edges; Explorer instrument is isometric subgraph of fused $G_{720720}$."

Trimming would be a stylistic call, not a structural fix. **Change from v2.7.3 to v2.7.4:**

The v2.7.4 change is the cyclotomic-substrate locator pass: substrate-level $\varphi$ on $\Gamma_5$, the non-collapse of $\varphi$ mechanisms, the Rogers-Ramanujan cousinhood observation, and the addition of Open Problem 30 (Galois-decorated FVLT orbits) as an out-of-kernel structural extension. No theorem changes; one new interpretive remark, one new appendix-K remark, one new note, one new Open Problem.

- New **Remark 9.3c** (Substrate-Level $\varphi$ on $\Gamma_5$) inserted in §9.3a. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Locates the pentagonal chord ratio $\varphi = (1+\sqrt{5})/2$ at the substrate level of the FVLT helix construction on $\Gamma_5$ — not as a derived numerical coincidence but as a geometric constant of the rational skeleton at the $\Gamma_5$ slice. Identifies $\Gamma_5$ as the carrier of the icosahedral / level-5 modular phenomena's *substrate*, distinct from the *orbit machine* that acts on it. Sets up the v2.7.6 Rmk 9.3d work (architectural content of the residue fan) by establishing the substrate-locator discipline.

- New **Note 9.6** (Rogers–Ramanujan cousinhood at the carrier spine) added. **Status: interpretive**, register: interpretive. Records the geometric cousinhood between FVLT helix construction on $\Gamma_5$ and the Rogers–Ramanujan continued-fraction CM evaluations such as $e^{-2\pi/5} R(e^{-2\pi}) = \sqrt{(5+\sqrt{5})/2} - (\sqrt{5}+1)/2$. The cousinhood is observed at the substrate level (shared $\Gamma_5$ geometric ancestor); orbit-machine identity is *not* claimed and is the subject of OP 30. Three concrete touchpoints listed: icosahedral / level-5 modular phenomena, $\varphi$-arrivals in $\mathbb{Z}[\varphi]$, Klein's icosahedral resolvent.

- New **Remark K.3d** (Non-collapse of $\varphi$ Mechanisms) inserted in Appendix K. **Status: interpretive guardrail**, register: interpretive. Records that numerical coincidence in $\mathbb{Z}[\varphi]$ is NOT structural identity. The substrate is shared (FVLT helix on $\Gamma_5$, Rogers–Ramanujan CM tower, icosahedral resolvent all reach $\mathbb{Z}[\varphi]$); the *orbit machines* that act on it are different ($\Gamma$-equivariance on the upper half-plane, Galois action on level-5 modular curves, $A_5 \cong \mathrm{PSL}(2, \mathbb{F}_5)$ representation theory, FVLT divisor-lattice gcd-orbits). This is a structural non-conflation guardrail preventing the substrate cousinhood from being read as theorem-identity.

- New **Open Problem 30** (Galois-decorated FVLT orbits) added to the Open Problems section as an *out-of-kernel structural extension* — explicitly recorded as not entered into the kernel as definition or theorem; no kernel theorem depends on it. Asks: does there exist a structural extension attaching class-field / Galois-conjugacy data to FVLT gcd-orbits, so that Galois action on cyclotomic and ring-class fields becomes a decoration on the divisor lattice rather than an external comparator? The Rogers–Ramanujan CM evaluation is the natural first test case. If no such extension exists, the obstruction should identify what distinguishes Galois orbit structure from FVLT gcd-orbit structure. (See full OP 30 text under §VII.7 of v3.0+ kernel / Open Problems list; transposed into the bibliography proper at v3.1.)

- No new external authorities, no theorem changes, no annotation register changes.

**Change from v2.7.2 to v2.7.3:**

The v2.7.3 changes are two unconditional remarks added to §8.4a, both clarifying existing theorem content rather than introducing new mathematics. Both originated from external review of the FVLT Lens Regime Explorer v3.1.6 by Muse Spark (Meta AI, May 3, 2026), audited and formalized by Claude and Chris/ChatGPT, through a metric-faithfulness test suite and a β/p_null diagnostic harness; session direction by Robin.

- New **Remark 8.20e.1** (Null-Model Validity: Surrogates Must Be Transform-Output, Not Arbitrary Energy Assignments) inserted between Rmk 8.20e and Def 8.20f. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. Sharpens the null-calibration requirement of Rmk 8.20e by separating two validity conditions: (N1) the candidate surrogate $\mathcal{E}_{\text{bridge}}$ must be the bridge restriction of an actual FVLT forward transform output $\mathcal{E}$ at $N_0 = 720720$, applied to a null-hypothesis input $x_{\text{null}}$; (N2) $x_{\text{null}}$ is generated by a procedure that destroys structure under test while preserving marginal statistics (typically permutation per Rmk 8.20e (i) or matched-density per (ii)). The structural diagnostic for (N1) is the monotone-filtration violation rate: by Thm 8.20a clause (ii), any bridge restriction of a transform output satisfies $\rho_k < \varepsilon \implies \rho_j < \varepsilon$ for $j > k$, so a nonzero violation rate at any $\varepsilon \in (0,1)$ proves the surrogate is outside the transform-output class. Independent draws of nonnegative values over the 37 channels — even when normalized — fail (N1) and produce monotone violations.
- New **Remark 8.20g.2** (Bridge Correspondences are Metric-Free Pairings, Not Hasse Edges) inserted between Cor 8.20g and Interpretive Rmk 8.20g.1. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. States that the §8.4a bridge architecture decomposes into two separable layers: a metric layer (the Explorer instrument graph $G_{\mathrm{Exp}} = G_{720} \cup_{\{1\}} G_{1001}$, 71 Hasse covers under Def 2.2b) and a correspondence layer (the eight totient pairings $c \leftrightarrow \varphi(c)$ for $c \in \text{Div}(1001)$, realizing $\varphi(1001) = 720 = 6!$ levelwise). The seven nontrivial pairs have kernel distances 3, 3, 4, 6, 7, 7, 10; insertion as unit edges would create seven metric violations. Each nontrivial pair is cross-domain (Def 2.2c), so by Cor 8.20g the bridge cost vanishes ($\Delta = 0$) — the totient layer is metrically free. Furthermore, $G_{\mathrm{Exp}}$ is an *isometric subgraph* of the full fused Hasse graph $G_{720720}$ (240 nodes, 832 covers): the 203 missing mixed-support nodes do not create shortcuts between Explorer endpoints. Includes terminology guardrail — "the bridge edges" reserved for the three atomic Hasse covers $1 \to \{7, 11, 13\}$ (the bridge interface); the eight §8.4a bridge objects are *bridge pairs*, *correspondences*, or *totient pairings*, not edges.
- §8.4a Key Features line in kernel header expanded to mention both new remarks.
- Document Summary updated: Duality bullet adds the bridge correspondence layer and metric-free language; Bridge filtration bullet adds the isometric-subgraph statement; Calibration bullet adds the null-validity criterion.
- No new external authorities. No new Open Problems. Both remarks formalize what was already implicit in Cor 8.20g and Thm 8.20a clause (ii) — the contribution is recording the structural facts as explicit architectural remarks usable as guardrails for instrument design and surrogate validity.
- Companion artifact: **FVLT Lens Regime Explorer v3.1.7** (Robin/Claude, May 3, 2026) — corrects v3.1.6's README block conflation of "8 bridge edges" by naming the eight totient correspondences explicitly with their kernel distances, citing Cor 8.20g for the metric-free property, and citing Test 3.5 for the isometric embedding result. Footer rephrased from "71 covers + 8 bridge (kernel spec)" to "71 Hasse covers + 8 totient correspondences (§8.4a)". No graph topology, layout, or computation altered — the visualization itself was correct as v3.1.6 rendered it; v3.1.7 fixes only the explanation of what the eight bridge objects are.
- New bibliography section: **Architecture Notes**. Categorically distinct from Worked Examples (which verify specific kernel propositions) and from external Reference sections (which cite outside authorities). Architecture Notes record framework-internal observations about FVLT's design properties — meta-observations about the structure of the framework itself. First entry [ANL-1] (Lattice-Legibility and Model-Auditable Mathematics) records the v2.7.3 session's observation that FVLT is unusually legible to LLMs at the surface and unusually auditable below it, with the four design features that produce this property (coordinates, computable metric, declared context switches, machine-readable status), the three-claim structure (legibility, fallibility, civic audit), and the closing line *"FVLT did not become model-legible by being simplified. It became model-legible by making its structure inspectable."* Status: methodological observation, not a theorem, not cited as authority for any kernel claim. **Use:** cite in discussions of model-readability of mathematical frameworks; do not cite as support for kernel theorems.
- Test artifacts produced during the v2.7.3 session: `test3_metric_faithfulness.py` (base-agnostic Hasse metric faithfulness for arbitrary Div(N)), `test3_v2_explorer_metric.py` (Explorer UI metric faithfulness for the rendered two-component graph), `test3_5_theorem_clean_audited.py` (theorem-clean bridge-layer separation: totient correspondence, not unit metric adjacency, plus isometric-embedding result for Explorer ↪ Div(720720)), `test_beta_null_calibration.py` (β / p_null harness from Thm 8.20a / Def 8.20b — committed as experimental diagnostic, not CI-gated, awaiting real FVLT forward transform), `test_beta_null_diagnostics.py` (ρ_k distribution diagnostics, monotone-filtration violation check, threshold sweep — sits on top of the calibration harness via same-directory import).

**Change from v2.6.2 to v2.7.0:**

The v2.7.0 change is the addition of **Remark 5.7a** (Coprime Indicator Decomposition) to §5.5 of the kernel — the closed-form Ramanujan-Fourier decomposition for the coprime-indicator signal $\chi_n$, providing the analytical anchor for ce_z calibration. The version bump is v2.6.2 → v2.7 (no patch suffix initially; later renumbered to v2.7.0 for consistency with subsequent v2.7.x series).

- New **Remark 5.7a** (Coprime Indicator Decomposition) inserted in §5.5. **Status: unconditional**, register: proved. The coprime indicator function $\chi_n(t) = \mathbb{1}[\gcd(t, n) = 1]$ on $\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}$ has the exact closed-form Ramanujan-Fourier expansion:
  $$E_q(\chi_n) = \frac{\mu(q)^2 \varphi(n)^2}{n^2 \varphi(q)}$$
  for $q \mid n$, with $E_q = 0$ for $q \nmid n$. The expansion is exact (not asymptotic) at $L = n$; convergence in $L \to \infty$ is unnecessary because the signal is supported exactly on $\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}$. The closed form depends on $n$ only through $\mathrm{rad}(n)$ (the radical / squarefree part) — radical-invariance: $E_q(\chi_n) = E_q(\chi_{\mathrm{rad}(n)})$ for all $q \mid \mathrm{rad}(n)$. Empirically verified: $\chi_{30}, \chi_{60}, \chi_{120}, \chi_{720}$ all yield the same concentration ratio $\mathrm{conc} = 4/11$.

- New **Three Reference Types** section added to SKILL.md (signal calibration discipline): *known-spectrum reference* (χₙ — analytic anchor for ce_z, this remark), *null reference* (noise — populates p_null), *diffusion baseline* (lawfully-distributed structured signals). With explicit warning: *"χₙ is maximally structured on the squarefree skeleton, not structureless; calling it 'null' inverts its calibration role."*

- Companion artifact: **`fvlt_fourier_comparison_v01.jsx`** (May 1, 2026; later: `.html` version) — the calibration chamber. Five presets (coprime / multi / goldbach / noise / sinusoid), two orthogonal verdicts (concentration + alignment), q=1 DC discipline, scope-honesty footer. Verifies the Rmk 5.7a closed form numerically and demonstrates radical-invariance.

- No new external authorities (HW08, V14a/b, FGK14 already cited at v2.6.2). No new Open Problems. No theorem changes.

**Three-stack attribution for v2.7.0:**
- **Robin** (architect — directed creation of the closed-form E_q(χ_n) anchor for ce_z calibration; specified the calibration chamber as companion artifact; required the Three Reference Types typology to prevent χ_n from being mislabeled as "null").
- **Claude / Anthropic** (drafted Rmk 5.7a closed-form derivation; produced empirical verification across four radicals; drafted `fvlt_fourier_comparison_v01.jsx`; SKILL.md Three Reference Types section).
- **Chris / ChatGPT / OpenAI** (three precision fixes applied: (a) name conflict resolution between intermediate naming candidates; (b) "known null" → "known-spectrum reference" — χ_n is analytic-anchor, not noise-floor; (c) "squarefree moduli" → "radicals" — the load-bearing dependence is on $\mathrm{rad}(n)$, not on squarefree-ness of $n$ itself).

**Change from v2.5 to v2.6:**
- New §8.4b (Complement Topology and the Selection of N₀ = 720): makes explicit the structural reason that S₆ is selected as the operating shell, refining Remark 8.18a's statement that S₆ is "the maximal finite shell admitting complete internal resolution" by identifying the topological mechanism. Sits between §8.4a (Internal Bridge Filtration) and §8.5; uses 8.20h-prefix labels (extending §8.4a's 8.20a–8.20g.1 sequence) to avoid collision with the existing Definition 8.21 (Generator Semigroup) at the head of §8.5. No existing labels renumbered.
  - Definition 8.20h (Proper Complement Boolean Lattice): introduces $B_r^\circ = B_r \setminus \{\hat 0, \hat 1\}$ for closure index r = |B|, and the order complex Δ(Bᵣ°). Concrete enumeration for S₂ (r = 1, B₁° empty), S₄ (r = 2, two singletons), S₆ (r = 3, three singletons + three doubletons).
  - Theorem 8.20h.1 (Topology of the Proper Complement Boolean Lattice): unconditional, classical. $\Delta(B_r^\circ) \simeq S^{r-2}$. Cites [Bjö95] and [Wac07] as external authorities; the framework's contribution is the recognition of the r = 3 threshold within the FVLT closure structure, not the topology theorem itself. Verification line cites [Mil26-WE1] for the explicit hexagonal-cycle computation at r = 3.
  - Remark 8.20h.2 (Loop Topology Threshold): r = 3 is the smallest closure index at which Δ(Bᵣ°) carries a 1-cycle. At r = 1 the complex is empty; at r = 2 it is S⁰ (two disconnected vertices, no cycle); at r = 3 it is S¹ (hexagonal cycle). For r ≥ 4 the 1-cycle would be a boundary within S^(r−2), but closure beyond r = 3 fails by Theorem 8.14a, so r = 3 is operatively unique within FVLT.
  - Theorem 8.20h.3 (Selection of N₀ = 720): unconditional, given Theorems 8.14a and 8.20h.1. S₆ is the smallest closed shell with closure index r ≥ 3, and is therefore the first closed factorial shell with complement loop topology. The bridge architecture in §8.4a requires the complement to support coupled loop dynamics, available only at r ≥ 3.
  - Remark 8.20h.4 (Maximality of S₆): unconditional. Combining Theorems 8.14a and 8.20h.3 gives the strengthened selection: S₆ is both the first and largest closed factorial shell with nontrivial complement topology. Refines Remark 8.18a's "maximal finite shell" claim by naming the topological reason.
  - Remark 8.20h.5 (CC29: Beyond the r = |B| Closure Rule): conditional. Cross-references Open Problem 29 (CC29) — the conjectural extension to closure rules with r ≠ |B|. Empirically verified for n ≤ 20 ([Mil26-WE2]); not entered into kernel as conjecture. Theorem 8.20h.3 does not depend on CC29.
  - Interpretive Remark 8.20h.6 (Polyhedral Analogue): records the geometric intuition — N₀ = 720 plays a role under totient-duality analogous to a sphere's role under polar reciprocation: not unique among arithmetic dualities but canonical with respect to the framework's construction order. Status: interpretive, not theorem-grade.
- New bibliography section: **Worked Examples and Internal Audit Notes**. Categorically distinct from literature references; entries are explicit framework-internal verifications archived with their reproducible computational basis. Two initial entries: [Mil26-WE1] (order complex of B₃°) and [Mil26-WE2] (factorial closure scan n = 2 to 20). Citation hygiene rule stated in section preamble: external authorities for general results remain cited from topical sections; internal audit notes are not authorities.
- Two new external authorities in **Combinatorial Topology**: [Bjö95] (Björner, "Topological methods," Handbook of Combinatorics vol. 2, 1995) and [Wac07] (Wachs, "Poset Topology: Tools and Applications," IAS/Park City lecture series, 2007). Both standard references for the homotopy type of the proper Boolean lattice's order complex; both cited from Theorem 8.20h.1's Verification line.
- Open Problem 29 added: **Closure Conjecture (CC29)** — Factorial initial closure beyond r = |B|. Asks whether the closure phenomenon admits any solutions when the constraint r = |B| is relaxed. Empirically verified for n ≤ 20 (no additional solutions). Asymptotic regime change at n = 7 supports the conjecture heuristically. Not used by any kernel proof.
- Document Summary "Duality" line extended: adds "selection of S₆ forced by complement loop topology at r = 3 (Thm 8.20h.3)" and theorem citations to Thm 8.14a.
- Document Summary "Status" line extended: notes that CC29 (Open Problem 29) is conjectural and is not used by any kernel proof.
- Three-stack attribution for v2.6: Robin (polyhedral observation that started the thread; observation that φ(C₀) returns the basis under totient — the self-returning identity), Claude (drafted closure section, made several audit failures including the "smallest squarefree product" misformulation and an over-stated topology claim, both caught and corrected; produced kernel section with proper label coordination after catching label collision in initial draft), Chris/ChatGPT (caught the smallest-squarefree-product error with two-minute counterexamples; corrected the topology claim by distinguishing the proper-part order complex from the full lattice; provided the "closure is not unique; loop topology selects" spine sentence that became the kernel selection principle).

**Change from v2.4 to v2.5:**
- New §1.5 (Walk-Relative Lens): formalizes the walk-relative measurement architecture gestured at by Remark 1.2a (Universal Lattice).
  - Definition 1.3 (Base Lens, Operative Lens, Forced Extension): distinguishes designed lens N_base from walk-derived operative lens N_t under extension rule E. Canonical extension rule is lcm-based over prime support of the walk state.
  - Remark 1.3a (Lens History as Measurement Record): extension events are observables available to any instrument that so records. Phrasing is deliberately permissive ("may be recorded") rather than commitment-making ("is part of") — symmetric to Remark 1.4c.
  - Remark 1.3b (Coordinate Embedding Under Extension): canonical zero-embedding of Div(N_t) into Div(N_{t+1}) when N_t | N_{t+1}; minimum coordinate discipline needed for persistent state to remain well-defined.
  - Remark 1.3c (Scope of Base-Lens Properties; Non-Lift Warning): **load-bearing.** Kirchhoff number, Fiedler value, S₆ symmetry, and factorization-specific theorems are properties of N_base and do not automatically lift to N_t. Prevents silent invalidation of base-specific constants at extended lenses.
  - Remark 1.3d (Lens Monotonicity; Implementation-Layer Consequences): operative lens grows and does not contract; state-space growth has implementation consequences deferred from kernel.
  - Definition 1.4 (Scope Function, Breathing Lens): introduces scope function Υ(t) as instrument-declared and breathing lens N_t* as the minimal containing lattice for currently-scoped signal. Symbol Υ (upsilon) chosen to avoid collision with skew σ(t) of Definition 3.9. Four canonical scope functions listed: single-value, sliding-window, mass-threshold, measurement-support.
  - Remark 1.4a (Non-Monotonicity of the Breathing Lens): N_t* may contract or expand between steps; contraction does not remove channels from N_t.
  - Remark 1.4b (Nesting): **load-bearing.** N_base | N_t* | N_t holds for all t. Three interpretive levels of walk state: inside natural range (N_t* = N_base), reached outside base but within prior experience (N_base ⊂ N_t* ⊆ N_t), genuinely new territory (N_t* growing alongside N_t).
  - Remark 1.4c (Contraction and Expansion as Observable Events): symmetric to Remark 1.3a; may be recorded as measurement output.
  - Remark 1.4d (Scope Function as Instrument Choice): Υ is not kernel-mandated; different instruments with identical trajectories may produce different N_t* sequences depending on their Υ declarations.
  - Remark 1.4e (State Persistence; Operations Project to Scope): **load-bearing.** Persistent state π_t is indexed by Div(N_t), not Div(N_t*); contraction of breathing lens is a view change, not erasure. Operations may project to Div(N_t*) as needed.
- New §5.1l (Divisor-Graded Adaptation Rate): extends scalar η to diagonal H = Diag(η_q).
  - Definition 5.1l: divisor-graded persistent-state update π_t = (I − H)·π_{t−1} + H·e(x_t), with η_q ∈ (0, 1] for each q ∈ Div(N). Scalar update of Definition 5.1g recovered at η_q ≡ η.
  - Remark 5.1m (Stability, by Construction): spectral radius ρ(I − H) < 1 under η_q > 0; upper bound η_q ≤ 1 preserves monotone EMA decay. The interval (0, 1] is strictly tighter than the asymptotic-stability range (0, 2).
  - Remark 5.1n (Fiedler-Timescale Reading of η_q; Reframes Open Problem 27): under channel-indexed rates, the Fiedler-timescale question becomes "how are the η_q structured across the lattice" rather than "what is η." Reframes rather than resolves OP 27.
  - Remark 5.1o (Endogenous Diagnostic Refinement): perturbation depth δ decomposes across orbits with per-orbit temporal structure — fast channel (large η_q) lagging vs. slow channel (small η_q) jittering are distinguishable diagnostic signatures.
- Open Problem 27 reframed per Remark 5.1n (see OP 27 entry for revised statement).

The implementation distinguishes accumulated lattice (prime→exponent Map, monotone) from current render lattice (lcm(N_base, walker value), freely varying) — exactly the architectural pattern §1.5 formalizes. Per audit discipline: the implementation is the source of the abstraction, not part of the kernel text.

## Attribution

The Ramanujan subspace $S_q$, its orthogonal projection, and the Ramanujan Periodic Transform for FIR signals are due to Vaidyanathan & Tenneti [V14a, V14b]. The FVLT applies this subspace decomposition to the divisor lattice of highly composite shells, organized by Stern-Brocot tree structure [Ste58, Bro61] and primorial boundaries. The lattice interpretation, HC shell selection (Definition 8.5d), primorial partitioning (Definition 8.5), E_q sliding scanner (Remark 8.5e), path-depth analysis, and all applications to financial, genomic, and physical time series are original to FVLT (Miller, 2026).

The geometric observation connecting the FVLT helix to the Guth-Maynard trilinear decomposition [GM24] — specifically, that the smooth-plus-spike structure of Dirichlet polynomial density diagrams corresponds to the E_q spectrum partitioned at primorial boundaries — was the catalyst for the E_q measurement layer (2024).

**New in v1.7 (Miller, 2026):**

- The Hasse instrument: divisor lattice as measurement basis (§1.4). Prior Ramanujan sum literature treats divisors as a flat frequency list; the FVLT exploits the lattice structure — partial order, covering relations, Ω-stratification, CRT fibers, primary-dual pairing — as the coordinate system of the transform.

> **Geometric prior art and the FVLT departure.** The visual study of cyclic supercharacters —
> plotting the *values* of Gaussian periods / cyclic supercharacters σ_X : ℤ/nℤ → ℂ in the complex
> plane and studying the geometry of the resulting image (hypocycloid envelope curves, residue-class
> colorings, composite-modulus structure) — was developed by Duke, Garcia, and Lutz [DGL15], Garcia,
> Hyde, and Lutz [GHL15], and Lutz [L19], extending the symmetric-group case [Br13] and the
> generalized-DFT / uncertainty-principle treatment [Br14]. The graphic supercharacter literature is
> the **nearest geometric prior art for complex-plane value-images of exponential sums**. **FVLT
> departs in object, not medium:** it embeds Div(N) itself as a navigable lattice / atlas substrate —
> the poset, positioned by the §9.7 coordinates (angle from the exponent vector, radius from Ω by the
> self-similar law), with Hasse covers and Ω-shells — while treating the supercharacters as the
> spectral measurement layer over that substrate rather than as the plotted object. [DGL15]–[L19] plot
> the value-image of a supercharacter (a point set, studied for its shape); FVLT plots the structure.
> Three further features have no counterpart in [DGL15]–[L19], which are multiplicative-only
> value-image studies: the **additive register** fused to the multiplicative lattice (cursor s ↔
> Div(s); the rosette contact gate, Im z₁ = 0 ⟺ N ∣ s), the **vernier** generative equation
> (k = N ± 1, the conjugate pair), and the navigable **atlas instrument** over one shared integer
> substrate. Accordingly: the geometric *move* — cyclotomic exponential sums rendered as complex-plane
> geometry, with residue coloring and envelope curves — is **not** original to FVLT [DGL15, GHL15,
> L19, Br13]; the **divisor-lattice embedding, the fused additive register, and the vernier generator**
> are (Miller, 2026).

> **Additive-address prior art and the FVLT departure.** Placing integers on a spiral or grid and
> reading number-theoretic structure off the geometry is a long tradition; two layers bear on FVLT.
> (i) *Prime density along paths* — the Ulam spiral [SUW64], the Sacks spiral [Sacks94], and the
> Hardy–Littlewood "Conjecture F" / Bateman–Horn account of why quadratic sequences are prime-rich
> [HL23, BH62; survey AFG20] — reads primes along additive / polynomial paths, the density governed by
> the local-at-each-prime obstruction product A = ∏_p (p − ω(p))/(p − 1). (ii) *Divisor structure on a
> spiral* — integer spirals colored by a divisor-FUNCTION value: divisor count τ(n) [Col, Wolf],
> distinct-prime-factor count ω(n) [GNS], sum-of-divisors / smoothness [Wolf, Smooth], composite
> shading d(x) with primes as the holes [DivPlot]. FVLT's rosette/cone shares the rotational
> integer-address grammar — nearer the Sacks/Archimedean layout than Ulam's square grid, the vernier
> phase k·s·π/N being a rotational advance per additive step — and the divisor-first stance (the
> Divisor Plot's "primes are holes in composite patterns" is the same inversion). **FVLT departs on two
> counts.** First, *scalar vs lattice*: layer (ii) colors each integer by a divisor SCALAR
> (τ, ω, σ, smoothness); FVLT opens the full divisor LATTICE Div(s) per integer — the poset, its Hasse
> covers, the §9.7 embedding, navigable — of which the scalar is only the shadow (ω is the count; Ω and
> the cone are the thing counted). Second, *the coupling*: **the plotted integer is the end of the
> readout in the spiral literature; in FVLT the additive cursor s is a door that opens the
> multiplicative atom Div(s)** (the contact gate Im z₁ = 0 ⟺ N ∣ s). The local-obstruction resonance
> with Hardy–Littlewood is real but is a resemblance of kind, not object: HL's ω(p) counts roots of a
> quadratic mod p (a prime-production obstruction on a polynomial sequence); FVLT's gate is a
> divisibility condition (does N divide s). Accordingly: the integer-spiral address geometry, the
> prime-density-along-paths readings, and the divisor-scalar colorings are **not** original to FVLT
> [SUW64, Sacks94, HL23, BH62, Col, DivPlot, GNS]; the **divisor-lattice opening per integer and the
> additive↔multiplicative coupling s ↔ Div(s)** are (Miller, 2026).

- Bridge Closure theorem (Theorem 8.14a): φ(Pr) = k! holds iff k ∈ {2, 4, 6}. S₆ is the terminal closed shell.

- Totient Descent theorem (Theorem 8.14b): the bridge identity holds because complement totients are 5-smooth, with product recovering k! exactly. The closure is an arithmetic fact about the relationship between consecutive primes' totients and the factorial function.

- Radical-free architecture (Remark 8.18b): the FVLT's radical-free design, present since the framework's inception (2011), operates through exponential sums on coprime classes rather than radical extraction. The architecture works in a different algebraic category from radical resolution.

- Spinor-tensor decomposition of the divisor lattice (§8.7): squarefree nodes (μ≠0) as spinor sector, non-squarefree (μ=0) as tensor sector. Möbius chirality grading. Bridge as spinor isomorphism. Double cover at 360→720.

- Clinamen and connection-like behavior (Remark 4.6a): Epicurean-spinor dictionary mapping helix↔spinor, chirality↔Weyl decomposition, clinamen↔connection (behavioral analogue — same profile, not formal identification), Kakeya gap↔Dirac mass term.

- Generalized shell architecture and multi-shell tiling (Remark 8.5f).

**New in v1.8 (Miller, 2026):**

- Internal bridge filtration (§8.4a): Theorem 8.20a establishes that the 720720 lattice contains all three bridge closures as internal symmetries, yielding a three-level coherence diagnostic from a single forward transform.

- Bridge depth β(x) ∈ {0,1,2,3} (Definition 8.20b): the deepest bridge closure level at which a signal exhibits coherent energy distribution across basis and complement sectors.

- Internalized bridge (Remark 8.20d): resolves the tension between S₆ terminal closure and 720720 extension — the bridge is not absent, it is internalized. Bridge closure is a docking interface, not a wall.

- Open Problem 18 (Outer automorphism and bridge duality): the conjecture that the bridge closure at S₆ is an arithmetic projection of the outer automorphism unique to S₆. *Partially closed (May 20–21, 2026): the bridge operator on Div(30030) is the syntheme σ_ant = (2,7)(3,11)(5,13), with transposition dual τ = (2,7) under the outer automorphism, canonical relative to the totient-bridge/Sylvester marking. Representative-level uniqueness remains 8-fold open.*

- Notation extensions: bridge pairs Br_k, bridge coherence ratio ρ_k, bridge depth β(x).

**New in v1.9 (Miller, 2026):**

- Trilattice metric (§3.4): Definition of the zeta-angle ζ(N₀) = arctanh(1/N₀) and Vernier ratio V(N₀) = (N₀+1)/(N₀−1) = e^{2ζ} as the hyperbolic step size of the Farey triple. The identity V = e^{2ζ} means the Vernier ratio is an exponential in the hyperbolic metric, not an additive offset.

- Residue symmetry (Theorem 3.7): N₋ ≡ −1 and N₊ ≡ +1 (mod d) for all d | N₀ with d > 1. This uniform ±1 structure across the entire divisor lattice is what makes the temporal gearbox covariant — the derivative is the same geometric operation at every measurement channel. The symmetry breaks at min(B') = 7, the first complement prime, where 721 ≡ 0 (mod 7). The break is the Vernier shear.

- Temporal gearbox (§3.6): Discrete covariant derivatives — tension τ (contraction), skew σ (expansion), shear S (symmetric), curvature κ (second derivative) — of E_q and β in ζ-scaled units. Möbius regime interpretation: |τ| > |σ| → contracting (μ = −1 territory), balanced → aligned (μ = 0), |σ| > |τ| → expanding (μ = +1 territory). Applied to bridge depth β, the curvature κ(β) detects Mitochondrial Extension buildup in real time.

- Convergence to calculus (Theorem 3.12): As k → ∞, ζ(k!) → 0, V(k!) → 1, and the discrete trilattice derivatives converge to continuous derivatives. The instrument does not approximate calculus; it becomes calculus in the factorial limit.

- Jet formalization (Remark 9.2a): The "Jet of order 1" label in §9.2 made precise with explicit fiber coordinate ζ(N₀) and derivative definitions. The Farey triple is a local jet bundle over the factorial spine.

- Open Problems 19–20: optimal regime threshold (is δ = V(N₀) − 1 the natural sensitivity?), zeta function emergence (is the trilattice metric a local evaluation of the Riemann zeta function's geometry on the factorial spine?).

**New in v2.0 (Miller, 2026):**

- Null-model calibration (Remark 8.20e): Bridge depth β alone is not diagnostic; raw β saturates on noise. The calibrated diagnostic is the pair (β, p_null) via permutation test. Empirical basis: hexameter report §5.3 (Miller 2026).
- Complement energy Z-score (Definition 8.20f): ce_z = (E_C − mean(E_C_null)) / std(E_C_null) — continuous alternative to binary β threshold.
- Open Problems 21–22: physical coupling constants at factorial depths; discrete Möbius chirality vs. continuous Poincaré spin.

**New in v2.1 (Miller, 2026):**

- Vernier degeneration theorem (Theorem 3.14): as k → ∞, the clinamen vanishes and the surviving object is the Fourier integral kernel.
- Degeneration trichotomy (Theorem 12.7): four limits strip different layers of structure; factorial tower locked abelian; Witt recovery only via prime limit.
- Register audit: Interpretive Notes distinguished from Remarks throughout.

- FVLT Decomposition (Theorem 5.5): four-part statement replacing old §5.3.

- Bridge energy distribution and exceptional-set bifurcation (Remark 5.5c): introduces bridge concentration index H_bridge = Σ(e_i/E_C)² resolving ce_z scalar into 8-dimensional bridge profile; diffuse vs. concentrated exceptional regimes motivated by [GM24]; testable σ = 3/4 prediction; affine averaging refinement.
- Renumbering: old Remark 5.5c → 5.5d (Coefficient vs. Subspace Energy); old Definition 5.5d → 5.5e (Ramanujan Subspace Energy); old Definition 5.5e → 5.5f (Windowed Subspace Energy).

**New in v2.3 (Miller, 2026):**

- Three-alpha disambiguation (Remark 5.1a): identifies three distinct roles of the α symbol — αAmp (amplitude exponent, uniform scaling), αTilt (spectral tilt, walk weighting), αGeom (Farey-helix deformation) — resolving a multi-year symbol collision traceable to GeoGebra origins. Chris (ChatGPT) audit, March 29, 2026.
- Cumulative walk (Definition 5.1b): formalizes the weighted partial sum S_W(K) = Σ k^{αTilt} · e^{iθ(k)} as a new mathematical object with phasor increments ΔZ, ΔC and walk weights w_k.
- Canonical projection rule (Remark 5.1c): diagnostics (E_q, β, ce_z) computed from increment series; cumulative sums used for rendering. Working rule for the instrument; exact functional for αTilt ≠ 0 is open (Problem 23).
- Supercharacter classification (Remark 5.4a): cites Fowler, Garcia, Karaali [FGK14] proving Ramanujan sums are supercharacters on cyclic groups. Classifies the FVLT measurement basis in combinatorial representation theory.
- Dirichlet-type weighting identification (Remark 5.4b): identifies the walk exponent αTilt = −s with zeta-like weighting regimes. Status: interpretive. Precision: zeta-like in weighting law, not a classical zeta or L-function.
- Selective phasor collapse (Definition 4.7a): at rational resonance spin values, one phasor's walk destructively self-interferes while the other survives. Throat locus at Farey-rational points in s/N space. Distinct from pinch points (Remark 4.7c). Status: empirical (Problem 24).
- Walk regime taxonomy (Remark 9.4a): three qualitative regimes — Helix (αTilt < 0), Circle (αTilt ≈ 0), Spiral (αTilt > 0). Regime determined by interaction of spin, mode, and αTilt, not αTilt alone. Suggestive correspondence to Poincaré isometry types is open (Problem 25).
- Farey boundary hypothesis (Interpretive Note 9.4b): negative αTilt as candidate Farey-boundary operator — rational descent toward low-denominator closure scaffold. Kill condition: weakened if negative αTilt produces only visual contraction with no change in channel structure.
- Poincaré metric analogy (now in Poincaré Metric section of this document): walk weight 1/k at αTilt = −1 structurally analogous to Poincaré metric 1/y. Interpretive status; limits of analogy stated; proof roadmap given.
- Open Problems 21–22 (first stated in v2.0 kernel, now added to bibliography): coupling constants at factorial depth; discrete–continuous chirality correspondence.
- Open Problems 23–25: Parseval for weighted increments; selective collapse condition; walk regime / Poincaré isometry map.
- Open Problems 26–27: ergodic projection convergence for irrational-spin walks; canonical adaptation rate η = λ₁(N).

**New in v2.4 (Miller, 2026):**

- Persistent state on the divisor lattice (§5.1a): full formalization of the memory structure referenced by Open Problems 26–27.
- Lattice energy state (Definition 5.1d): snapshot vector e(k) recording where energy lands across the divisor lattice at each walk step.
- Ergodic projection (Definition 5.1e): Cesàro time-average π(K) — the theoretical reference object that characterizes stationary input.
- Orbit coarsening (Remark 5.1f): persistent state indexed by orbits under (ℤ/Nℤ)× rather than by elements of ℤ/Nℤ; the orbit is the natural grain of memory. Dual indexing convention (d ↔ q = N/d) documented.
- Persistent state update (Definition 5.1g): EMA filter π_t = (1−η)π_{t−1} + η e(x_t) as the operational memory. Three-alpha interaction with η mapped out.
- Cesàro vs. EMA distinction (Remark 5.1g-a): theoretical (Cesàro) and operational (EMA) objects should not be conflated; under regime change they diverge, with EMA tracking at timescale τ_η = 1/η.
- Relaxation rate and Fiedler value (Remark 5.1h): interpretive. λ₁ = 1/φ² sets natural comparison timescale τ_relax = φ² under diffusion-on-lattice model (assumed, not derived from walk dynamics).
- Perturbation depth (Definition 5.1i): δ = ‖e − π‖₂ as lattice-level analogue of surprise; orbit-decomposed.
- The orbit is the memory (Remark 5.1j): walk geometry is rendering, lattice projection is measurement. FVLT persistent state ↔ recurrent network hidden state analogy with the crucial disanalogy that FVLT's "weight matrix" is not learned.
- Endogenous diagnostics (Remark 5.1k): interpretive. Proprioceptive analogy at energy-distribution level — the instrument can measure its own displacement and localize perturbation by shell and domain. Does not extend to walk engine, Vernier phasors, spin, or mode machinery; not a claim about experience or consciousness.
- Variational completion of the walk instrument (§5.6): proposed structure. Discrete FVLT action S_N[w, λ; α], coherence constraint, FVLT momentum P_n(α), discrete Euler-Lagrange equation, shell-weight ψ_N(q) = log(q/√N), calibration identity. Proposed rather than established; provides variational framing for the walk instrument.
- Three-stack attribution: Claude (structural drafting of §5.1a and §5.6), Chris/ChatGPT (audit — Cesàro vs EMA distinction, Fiedler language tightened to interpretive, normalization qualifier, N-generic Laplacian replacing hardcoded FIEDLER_720), Robin (session direction — orbit-is-memory framing, §5.1a as bridge to Serena walk engine).

**New in v2.5 (Miller, 2026):**

- Walk-relative lens architecture (§1.5): formalizes the measurement-apparatus-as-moving-target pattern gestured at by Remark 1.2a. Distinguishes base lens N_base (designed), operative lens N_t (cumulative, monotone non-decreasing, records experience), and breathing lens N_t* (active, freely varying, records current scope). The kernel's primary object is no longer implicitly a fixed lattice; it is a triple (N_base, N_t, N_t*) with specified nesting N_base | N_t* | N_t.
- Base lens / operative lens / forced extension (Definition 1.3): operative lens derived from walk history via extension rule E; lcm-based extension over prime support is canonical. Forced extension events are observable.
- Non-lift warning for base-lens properties (Remark 1.3c): load-bearing kernel discipline. Kirchhoff number, Fiedler value, S₆ symmetry, and factorization-specific theorems are properties of N_base and do not automatically lift to N_t under extension. Without this remark, base-specific constants would be silently misapplied at extended lenses.
- Scope function and breathing lens (Definition 1.4): scope function Υ(t) is instrument-declared, not kernel-fixed. Four canonical scope functions listed. Symbol choice Υ deliberately avoids collision with skew σ(t) of Definition 3.9.
- Nesting N_base | N_t* | N_t (Remark 1.4b): load-bearing structural claim. Three interpretive levels of walk state — inside natural range (N_t* = N_base), reached outside base but within prior experience (N_base ⊂ N_t* ⊆ N_t), and genuinely new territory (N_t* growing alongside N_t).
- State persistence with scope projection (Remark 1.4e): load-bearing. Persistent state π_t is indexed by Div(N_t); contraction of breathing lens is a view change, not erasure. Operations may project to Div(N_t*) as needed. Without this remark, contraction of the breathing lens would create an incoherent state-discipline.
- Divisor-graded adaptation rate (Definition 5.1l): extends scalar η to diagonal H = Diag(η_q) with η_q ∈ (0, 1]. Enables per-orbit temporal structure in persistent state update.
- Reframing of Open Problem 27 (Remark 5.1n): the Fiedler-timescale question promoted from "what is η" to "how are the η_q structured across the lattice." Reframes rather than resolves; explicit derivation of η_q from λ₁ remains open.
- Open Problem 28 added: walk-relative lens interaction with adaptation rate — when N_t extends, should existing η_q be held fixed or recomputed?
- Collatz implementation (CollatzLattice_3.html, Miller, April 2026) identified as the implementation artifact that earned the breathing-lens abstraction. The HTML distinguishes accumulated lattice (prime→exponent Map, monotone) from current render lattice (lcm(N_base, walker value), freely varying) — exactly the architectural pattern §1.5 formalizes. Per audit discipline: implementation is the source of the abstraction, not part of kernel text.
- Three-stack attribution: Claude (kernel prose drafting of §1.5 and §5.1l; formalization of Collatz implementation pattern into abstract walk-relative architecture; η_q extension drafted and audit-revised), Chris/ChatGPT (audit passes — symmetric softening of Rmks 1.3a/1.4c from "is part of" to "may be recorded as part of"; η_q bound correction from (0,2) to (0,1] distinguishing EMA-behaved range from asymptotic-stability range; confirmation of Rmk 1.3c as load-bearing non-lift warning; approval of dual-lens architecture as two distinct kernel ideas rather than redundant framings), Robin (session direction — Collatz CollatzLattice_3.html as implementation earning the abstraction; contraction question that prompted §1.Y/Def 1.4; promotion of Open Problem 27 as session goal).
